## Dedication / 题献

===Original===
To 

 My Mother

===Chinese===
献给

我的母亲

## I / 第一章

===Original===
When an American sets out to found a college, he hunts first for a hill. John Harvard was an Englishman and indifferent to high places. The result is that Harvard has become a university of vast proportions and no color. Yale flounders about among the New Haven shops, trying to rise above them. The Harkness Memorial tower is successful; otherwise the university smells of trade. If Yale had been built on a hill, it would probably be far less important and much more interesting.

Hezekiah Sanford was wise; he found first his hill and then founded his college, believing probably that anyone ambitious enough to climb the hill was a man fit to wrestle with learning and, if need be, with Satan himself. Satan was ever before Hezekiah, and he fought him valiantly, exorcising him every morning in chapel and every evening at prayers. The first students of Sanford College learned Latin and Greek and to fear the devil. There are some who declare that their successors learn less.

Hezekiah built Sanford Hall, a fine Georgian building, performed the duties of trustees, president, dean, and faculty for thirty years, and then passed to his reward, leaving three thousand acres, his library of five hundred books, mostly sermons, Sanford Hall, and a charter that opened the gates of Sanford to all men so that they might “find the true light of God and the glory of Jesus in the halls of this most liberal college.”

===Chinese===
美国人着手创办一所大学时，头一件事是找一座小山。约翰·哈佛（John Harvard）是个英国人，对高地毫不在意。结果，哈佛成了一所规模宏大而毫无特色的大学。耶鲁（Yale）在纽黑文（New Haven）的店铺堆里扑腾，想从中间拔起身来。哈克尼斯纪念塔（Harkness Memorial tower）算是成功了；除此之外，整所大学一股生意气。耶鲁当年若是建在山上，它多半远不会这么显赫，却会有意思得多。

希西家·桑福德（Hezekiah Sanford）就有智慧；他先找到自己的山，然后才创办自己的学院。他多半相信：凡志气够大、肯爬上这座山的人，便是配同学问角力、必要时也配同撒旦本人角力的人。撒旦时时都在希西家眼前，他英勇地与之搏斗，每天清晨在礼拜堂里、每晚祷告时将它驱走。桑福德学院（Sanford College）最初的学生学拉丁文和希腊文，也学会敬畏魔鬼。有些人扬言，他们的后继者学到的更少了。

希西家建起了桑福德楼（Sanford Hall），一座漂亮的乔治王朝式建筑；又把董事、校长、教务长和全体教师的职分一肩挑了三十年，然后升天领赏去了，留下三千英亩土地、一座五百册藏书的图书室（大半是布道书）、桑福德楼，还有一纸特许状——它向天下人敞开桑福德的大门，好叫他们“在这座最为开明的学院的殿堂里，寻得上帝的真光与耶稣的荣耀”。

===Original===
More than a century had passed since Hezekiah was laid to rest in Haydensville’s cemetery. The college had grown miraculously and changed even more miraculously. Only the hill and its beautiful surroundings remained the same. Indian Lake, on the south of the campus, still sparkled in the sunlight; on the east the woods were as virgin as they had been a hundred and fifty years before. Haydensville, still only a village, surrounded the college on the west and north.

Hezekiah’s successors had done strange things to his campus. There were dozens of buildings now surrounding Sanford Hall, and they revealed all the types of architecture popular since Hezekiah had thundered his last defiance at Satan. There were fine old colonial buildings, their windows outlined by English ivy; ponderous Romanesque buildings made of stone, grotesque and hideous; a pseudo-Gothic chapel with a tower of surpassing loveliness; and four laboratories of the purest factory design. But despite the conglomerate and sometimes absurd architecture﻿—a Doric temple neighbored a Byzantine mosque﻿—the campus was beautiful. Lawns, often terraced, stretched everywhere, and the great elms lent a dignity to Sanford College that no architect, however stupid, could quite efface.

This first day of the new college year was glorious in the golden haze of Indian summer. The lake was silver blue, the long reflections of the trees twisting and bending as a soft breeze ruffled the surface into tiny waves. The hills already brilliant with color﻿—scarlet, burnt orange, mauve, and purple﻿—flamed up to meet the clear blue sky; the elms softly rustled their drying leaves; the white houses of the village retreated coyly behind maples and firs and elms: everywhere there was peace, the peace that comes with strength that has been stronger than time.

===Chinese===
希西家安葬在海登斯维尔（Haydensville）墓园里，至今已过一个多世纪。学院奇迹般地长大了，又以更大的奇迹改了模样。只有那座山和山四周的美景一如往昔。校园以南的印第安湖（Indian Lake）依旧在阳光下粼粼闪光；东面的树林，还像一百五十年前一样未经砍伐。海登斯维尔依旧只是个村庄，在西面和北面环抱着学院。

希西家的后继者们把他的校园折腾出了种种名堂。如今围绕着桑福德楼立起了几十座建筑，自希西家向撒旦吼出最后一声宣战之后流行起来的各种建筑样式，应有尽有。有精美的殖民时期老楼，窗棂爬满英国常春藤；有石头砌的笨重罗马式大楼，怪诞而丑陋；有一座仿哥特式礼拜堂，塔楼可爱得无以复加；还有四座最地道的厂房式设计的实验室。然而尽管建筑杂糅、时而荒唐——一座多立克式神庙与一座拜占庭式清真寺比邻而居——校园仍是美的。草坪处处铺展，往往层层叠成梯台，高大的榆树赋予桑福德学院一种气派，任凭多蠢的建筑师也抹除不尽。

新学年的第一天，辉耀在印第安夏（Indian summer）金色雾霭里。湖水是银蓝色的，清风把水面吹皱成细浪，树木长长的倒影便扭动弯曲起来。群山已烧出一片绚烂——猩红、焦橙、淡紫、深紫——腾起火焰，迎向澄净的蓝天；榆树让渐干的叶子簌簌作响；村里的白房子娇怯地躲进枫树、冷杉和榆树后面：到处是一派安宁，那是随着比时间更经久的力量而来的安宁。

===Original===
As Hugh Carver hastened up the hill from the station, his two suitcases banged his legs and tripped him. He could hardly wait to reach the campus. The journey had been intolerably long﻿—Haydensville was more than three hundred miles from Merrytown, his home﻿—and he was wild to find his room in Surrey Hall. He wondered how he would like his roommate, Peters.﻿ ﻿… What’s his name? Oh, yes, Carl.﻿ ﻿… The registrar had written that Peters had gone to Kane School.﻿ ﻿… Must be pretty fine. Ought to be first-class to room with.﻿ ﻿… Hugh hoped that Peters wouldn’t think that he was too country.﻿ ﻿…

Hugh was a slender lad who looked considerably less than his eighteen years. A gray cap concealed his sandy brown hair, which he parted on the side and which curled despite all his brushing. His crystalline blue eyes, his small, neatly carved nose, his sensitive mouth that hid a shy and appealing smile, were all very boyish. He seemed young, almost pathetically young.

People invariably called him a nice boy, and he didn’t like it; in fact, he wanted to know how they got that way. They gave him the pip, that’s what they did. He guessed that a fellow who could run the hundred in 10:2 and out-box anybody in high school wasn’t such a baby. Why, he had overheard one of the old maid teachers call him sweet. Sweet! Cripes, that old hen made him sick. She was always pawing him and sticking her skinny hands in his hair. He was darn glad to get to college where there were only men teachers.

===Chinese===
休·卡弗（Hugh Carver）从车站一路急急上山，两只手提箱磕着腿、绊着脚。他简直等不及走到校园。旅途长得难以忍受——海登斯维尔离他的家乡梅里敦（Merrytown）三百多英里——他心急火燎，只想找到他在萨里楼（Surrey Hall）的房间。他不知道自己会同室友彼得斯处得怎样。……他叫什么来着？哦，对了，卡尔。……注册主任来函说，彼得斯上的是凯恩中学（Kane School）。……想必呱呱叫。同他同住一屋该是一流的。……休只巴望彼得斯别嫌他太乡气。……

休是个清瘦少年，看上去比他十八岁的年纪还要小好些。一顶灰帽子罩着他沙棕色的头发——他把它偏分，可无论怎么梳理，发梢总要打卷。他那双水晶般清澈的蓝眼睛、小巧而轮廓端正的鼻子、含着羞怯动人微笑的敏感的嘴，无一不带着孩子气。他显得年轻，年轻得近乎叫人可怜。

人们开口闭口总叫他“好孩子”，他可不喜欢；说实在的，他倒想知道他们是怎么落下这毛病的。他们叫他心里发烦，就是这么回事。他想，一个百码能跑 10 秒 2、中学里拳击谁都能打赢的家伙，总不至于还是个奶娃娃吧。哼，他还偷听过一位老处女教师说他“乖”呢。乖！见鬼，那只老母鸡叫他恶心。她总对他动手动脚，把干瘦的手指插进他头发里。总算上了大学，这儿清一色都是男教师，他可乐坏了。

===Original===
Women always wanted to get their hands into his hair, and boys liked him on sight. Many of those who were streaming up the hill before and behind him, who passed him or whom he passed, glanced at his eager face and thought that there was a guy they’d like to know.

An experienced observer would have divided those boys into three groups: preparatory school boys, carelessly at ease, well dressed, or, as the college argot has it, “smooth”; boys from city schools, not so well dressed perhaps, certainly not so sure of themselves; and country boys, many of them miserably confused and some of them clad in Kollege Kut Klothes that they would shamefacedly discard within a week.

Hugh finally reached the top of the hill, and the campus was before him. He had visited the college once with his father and knew his way about. Eager as he was to reach Surrey Hall, he paused to admire the pseudo-Gothic chapel. He felt a little thrill of pride as he stared in awe at the magnificent building. It had been willed to the college by an alumnus who had made millions selling rotten pork.

===Chinese===
女人们总想把手伸进他的头发，男孩子们却是一见就喜欢他。在他身前身后涌上山来的那许多人里，走过的、被他赶上的，都会瞥一眼他那张热切的脸，心想：这小子值得结识。

眼光老练的观察者会把那些小伙子分成三拨：预科学校出来的，从容闲适，衣着讲究——用大学里的行话来说，“光鲜”；城市公立学校出来的，衣着兴许不及前者，底气也肯定不及前者；还有乡下来的，其中许多个晕头转向得可怜，有几个则从头到脚裹着“学院剪裁”（Kollege Kut Klothes，店家故意弄出的花哨拼法）牌衣裳，不出一个礼拜，他们就要臊眉耷眼地把这些衣裳扔到一边。

休终于登上了山顶，校园在他面前铺展开来。他从前随父亲来参观过一回，认得路径。虽恨不得一步跨进萨里楼，他还是停了停，欣赏那座仿哥特式礼拜堂。他敬畏地凝望着这座堂皇的建筑，心头掠过一丝小小的得意。它是一位靠卖变质猪肉挣下几百万的校友遗赠给学院的。

===Original===
Hugh skirted two of the factory laboratories, hurried between the Doric temple and Byzantine mosque, paused five times to direct confused classmates, passed a dull red colonial building, and finally stood before Surrey Hall, a large brick dormitory half covered by ivy.

He hurried upstairs and down a corridor until he found a door with 19 on it. He knocked.

“What th’ hell! Come in.” The voice was impatiently cheerful.

===Chinese===
他绕过两座厂房式实验室，从多立克式神庙和拜占庭式清真寺中间疾步穿过，五次停下脚来给摸不着头脑的同学指路，经过一幢暗红色的殖民时期楼房，终于站到了萨里楼跟前——那是一大片砖砌宿舍，半壁爬满常春藤。

他急急上了楼，沿一条走廊一路找过去，直到看见一扇门上标着 19 号。他敲了敲门。

“搞什么鬼！进来。”那嗓音不耐烦里透着兴头。

===Original===
Hugh pushed open the door and entered the room to meet wild confusion﻿—and his roommate. The room was a clutter of suitcases, trunks, clothes, banners, unpacked furniture, pillows, pictures, golf-sticks, tennis-rackets, and photographs﻿—dozens of photographs, all of them of girls apparently. In the middle of the room a boy was on his knees before an open trunk. He had sleek black hair, parted meticulously in the center, a slender face with rather sharp features and large black eyes that almost glittered. His lips were full and very red, almost too red, and his cheeks seemed to be colored with a hard blush.

“Hullo,” he said in a clear voice as Hugh came in. “Who are you?”

Hugh flushed slightly. “I’m Carver,” he answered, “Hugh Carver.”

===Chinese===
休推开门走进房间，迎面撞上一片狂乱的狼藉——还有他的室友。屋里乱堆着手提箱、大衣箱、衣裳、锦旗、没拆开的家具、枕头、图画、高尔夫球杆、网球拍，还有照片——几十张照片，看模样全是姑娘。屋子正中，一个少年跪在一只敞开的衣箱前。他一头乌黑油亮的头发，一丝不苟地中分；瘦长的脸，五官颇锋利，一双黑色大眼睛几乎灼灼放光。他嘴唇丰厚而鲜红，红得几乎过了分，两颊又仿佛敷着一层浓浓的绯红。

“喂，”休进来时，他声音清亮地说，“你是哪位？”

休微微红了脸。“我是卡弗，”他答道，“休·卡弗。”

===Original===
The other lad jumped to his feet, revealing, to Hugh’s surprise, golf knickers. He was tall, slender, and very neatly built.

“Hell!” he exclaimed. “I ought to have guessed that.” He held out his hand. “I’m Carl Peters, the guy you’ve got to room with﻿—and God help you.”

Hugh dropped his suitcases and shook hands. “Guess I can stand it,” he said with a quick laugh to hide his embarrassment. “Maybe you’ll need a little of God’s help yourself.” Diffident and unsure, he smiled﻿—and Peters liked him on the spot.

===Chinese===
那少年一跃而起，叫休吃惊的是，他下身是一条高尔夫灯笼裤。他个子高挑，清瘦，身段十分匀称。

“嘿！”他叫道，“我本该猜到的。”他伸出手。“我是卡尔·彼得斯（Carl Peters），跟你同屋的家伙——愿上帝保佑你。”

休撂下手提箱，同他握手。“我想我还受得住，”他急忙笑了一声，遮掩自己的窘态，“没准你自己也用得着上帝保佑呢。”他怯生生的、没把握地微笑着——彼得斯当下就喜欢上了他。

===Original===
“Chase yourself,” Peters said easily. “I know a good guy when I see one. Sit down somewhere﻿—er, here.” He brushed a pile of clothes off a trunk to the floor with one sweep of his arm. “Rest yourself after climbing that goddamn hill. Christ! It’s a bastard, that hill is. Say, your trunk’s downstairs. I saw it. I’ll help you bring it up soon’s you’ve got your wind.”

Hugh was rather dazzled by the rapid, staccato talk, and, to tell the truth, he was a little shocked by the profanity. Not that he wasn’t used to profanity; he had heard plenty of that in Merrytown, but he didn’t expect somehow that a college man﻿—that is, a prep-school man﻿—would use it. He felt that he ought to make some reply to Peters’s talk, but he didn’t know just what would do. Peters saved him the trouble.

“I’ll tell you, Carver﻿—oh, hell, I’m going to call you Hugh﻿—we’re going to have a swell joint here. Quite the darb. Three rooms, you know; a bedroom for each of us and this big study. I’ve brought most of the junk that I had at Kane, and I s’pose you’ve got some of your own.”

===Chinese===
“去你的，”彼得斯满不在乎地说，“是不是好家伙，我一眼就看得出来。找地方坐——呃，坐这儿。”他胳膊一扫，把衣箱上的一堆衣裳全扒拉到地上。“爬完那座混账山，歇口气吧。基督啊！那山真是个狗娘养的东西。我说，你的大衣箱在楼下，我瞧见了。等你喘匀了气，我帮你搬上来。”

这急管繁弦般的一连串话让休听得有些发晕；说老实话，那些粗口也让他微微吃惊。倒不是他听不得粗口；在梅里敦他听得多了去了，只是不知怎么，他没料到一个大学生——也就是说，一个预科学校出身的人——也会讲粗口。他觉得总该回应彼得斯两句，却又不知道说什么才合适。彼得斯替他省了这个麻烦。

“我跟你说，卡弗——哦，见鬼，我就叫你休吧——咱们这儿将要是个气派的好地方。顶呱呱的。三间屋，知道吗；咱们一人一间卧室，外加这间大书房。我在凯恩中学的大部分家什都搬来了，我寻思你自己也带了些吧。”

===Original===
“Not much,” Hugh replied, rather ashamed of what he thought might be considered stinginess. He hastened to explain that he didn’t know what Carl would have; so he thought that he had better wait and get his stuff at college.

“That’s the bean,” exclaimed Carl, He had perched himself on the window-seat. He threw one well shaped leg over the other and gazed at Hugh admiringly. “You certainly used the old bean. Say, I’ve got a hell of a lot of truck here, and if you’d a brought much, we’d a been swamped.﻿ ﻿… Say, I’ll tell you how we fix this dump.” He jumped up, led Hugh on a tour of the rooms, discussed the disposal of the various pieces of furniture with enormous gusto, and finally pointed to the photographs.

“Hope you don’t mind my harem,” he said, making a poor attempt to hide his pride.

===Chinese===
“没带多少，”休答道，暗自担心人家会当成小气，颇有些讪讪的。他赶紧解释说，他不知道卡尔会置办些什么，所以想还是等到了学校再添置自己的东西为好。

“这就对了！”卡尔叫道。他已在窗座上落了座，一条线条漂亮的长腿往另一条上一翘，赞赏地端详着休。“你算是真动过脑子。我说，我这儿的破烂家什多得吓人，你要是也带上一大堆，咱们可就淹死了。……我说，我来讲讲这破窝怎么拾掇。”他一跃而起，领着休把几间屋子巡了一遍，兴致勃勃地商量各件家具的去处，末了指了指那些照片。

“希望你不见怪我的‘后宫’佳丽，”他说，想把得意藏起来，却藏得很不高明。

===Original===
“It’s some harem,” replied Hugh in honest awe.

Again he felt ashamed. He had pictures of his father and mother, and that was all. He’d write to Helen for one right away. “Where’d you get all of ’em? You’ve certainly got a collection.”

“Sure have. The album of hearts I’ve broken. When I’ve kissed a girl twice I make her give me her picture. I’ve forgotten the names of some of these janes. I collected ten at Bar Harbor this summer and three at Christmas Cove. Say, this kid﻿—” he fished through a pile of pictures﻿—“was the hottest little devil I ever met.” He passed to Hugh a cabinet photograph of a standard flapper. “Pet? My God!” He cast his eyes ceilingward ecstatically.

===Chinese===
“好一群‘后宫’佳丽，”休答道，满心实打实的惊叹。

他又一次害臊了。他手头只有父亲和母亲的照片，再无其他。他得马上写信向海伦（Helen）要一张。“你这些是从哪儿弄来的？你可是真攒了一大收藏啊。”

“那还用说。这本是我踩碎的心的集子。哪个姑娘我亲过两回，就叫她送我一张照片。这里头几个小妞的名字我都忘了。今年夏天我在巴尔港（Bar Harbor）收了十张，在克里斯马斯科夫（Christmas Cove）又收了三张。我说，这个小东西——”他在一堆照片里翻拣着，“是我这辈子碰上过的最辣的小妖精。”他递给休一张柜式相片，上面是个标准款式的轻佻女郎（flapper）。“小可人儿？我的老天爷！”他心醉神迷地把眼睛翻向天花板。

===Original===
Hugh’s mind was a battlefield of disapproval and envy. Carl dazzled and confused him. He had often listened to the recitals of their exploits by the Merrytown Don Juans, but this good-looking, sophisticated lad evidently had a technique and breadth of experience quite unknown to Merrytown. He wanted badly to hear more, but time was flying and he hadn’t even begun to unpack.

“Will you help me bring up my trunk?” he asked half shyly.

“Oh, hell, yes. I’d forgotten all about that. Come on.”

===Chinese===
休的头脑成了一片战场，不以为然与艳羡厮杀不休。卡尔叫他眼花缭乱、心神不定。梅里敦的唐璜们夸耀自己的风流战绩，他没少在旁边听；可这个漂亮、老成的少年，分明有一套梅里敦闻所未闻的手段与阅历。他巴不得再多听些，无奈光阴似箭，他连行李都还没动手拆。

“你帮我把我那口箱子搬上来，行吗？”他半带羞怯地问。

“哦，见鬼，行啊。这茬我全忘了。走吧。”

===Original===
They spent the rest of the afternoon unpacking, arranging and rearranging the furniture and pictures. They found a restaurant and had dinner. Then they returned to 19 Surrey and rearranged the furniture once more, pausing occasionally to chat while Carl smoked. He offered Hugh a cigarette. Hugh explained that he did not smoke, that he was a sprinter and that the coaches said that cigarettes were bad for a runner.

“Right-o,” said Carl, respecting the reason thoroughly. “I can’t run worth a damn myself, but I’m not bad at tennis﻿—not very good, either. Say, if you’re a runner you ought to make a fraternity easy. Got your eye on one?”

“Well,” said Hugh, “my father’s a Nu Delt.”

===Chinese===
那天下午余下的时光，他们都花在拆行李、摆弄家具和照片上，摆了又换，换了又摆。他们找到一家饭馆吃了晚饭。随后回到萨里楼 19 室，又把家具重摆了一遍，偶尔停下来闲聊几句，卡尔抽着烟。他敬休一支香烟。休解释说，自己不抽烟，自己是短跑选手，教练们说香烟对跑短跑的人有害。

“好咧，”卡尔说，对这理由十二分地尊重。“我自己跑起步来一塌糊涂，网球倒还不赖——也说不上顶好。我说，你要是吃跑步这碗饭的，进兄弟会准容易。看中哪一家了没？”

“嗯，”休说，“我父亲是‘纽德尔塔’（Nu Delt）兄弟会的。”

===Original===
“The Nu Delts. Phew! High-hat as hell.” He looked at Hugh enviously. “Say, you certainly are set. Well, my old man never went to college, but I want to tell you that he left us a whale of a lot of jack when he passed out a couple of years ago.”

“What!” Hugh exclaimed, staring at him in blank astonishment.

In an instant Carl was on his feet, his flashing eyes dimmed by tears. “My old man was the best scout that ever lived﻿—the best damned old scout that ever lived.” His sophistication was all gone; he was just a small boy, heartily ashamed of himself and ready to cry. “I want you to know that,” he ended defiantly.

===Chinese===
“‘纽德尔塔’。嚯！傲气冲天。”他眼热地瞧着休。“我说，你算是板上钉钉的了。哼，我老子没上过大学，可我告诉你，两年前他过世的时候，给我们留下好大一笔钱。”

“什么！”休惊呼起来，目瞪口呆地望着他。

一眨眼卡尔已经跳了起来，炯炯放光的眼睛蒙上了泪水。“我老子是天底下头一号的好汉——天底下头一号顶顶好的老好汉。”他那副老练世故荡然无存；他只是个小男孩，为自己羞愧得不行，眼看就要哭出来。“我要你知道这一点。”他倔头倔脑地收了尾。

===Original===
At once Hugh was all sympathy. “Sure, I know,” he said softly. Then he smiled and added, “So’s mine.”

Carl’s face lost its lugubriousness in a broad grin. “I’m a fish,” he announced. “Let’s hit the hay.”

“You said it!”

===Chinese===
休顿时满心怜惜。“当然，我知道，”他轻声说。随即他微微一笑，添了一句：“我父亲也是。”

卡尔脸上的愁苦一扫而空，咧开大嘴笑了。“我真是条傻鱼，”他宣布道，“睡觉去吧。”

“可不是嘛！”

## II / 第二章

===Original===
Hugh wrote two letters before he went to bed, one to his mother and father and the other to Helen Simpson. His letter to Helen was very brief, merely a request for her photograph.

Then, his mind in a whirl of excitement, he went to bed and lay awake dreaming, thinking of Carl, the college, and, most of all, of Helen and his walk with her the day before.

He had called on her to say goodbye. They had been “going together” for a year, and she was generally considered his girl. She was a pretty child with really beautiful brown hair, which she had foolishly bobbed, lively blue eyes, and an absurdly tiny snub nose. She was little, with quick, eager hands﻿—a shallow creature who was proud to be seen with Hugh because he had been captain of the high-school track team. But she did wish that he wasn’t so slow. Why, he had kissed her only once, and that had been a silly peck on the cheek. Perhaps he was just shy, but sometimes she was almost sure that he was “plain dumb.”

===Chinese===
休上床之前写了两封信，一封给他的母亲和父亲，另一封给海伦·辛普森（Helen Simpson）。给海伦的那封十分简短，只不过是向她要一张照片。

随后，他脑子里兴奋得乱成一团，上了床，睁着眼睛躺着遐想；他想着卡尔，想着大学，而想得最多的，是海伦，还有前一天同她一起散步的情景。

前一天，他是去她家道别的。他们“相好”已经一年，大家多半把她看作他的姑娘。她是个漂亮的姑娘，一头真正漂亮的棕色头发——却叫她傻乎乎地剪短了——一双活泼的蓝眼睛，一只小得可笑的翘鼻子。她个子娇小，一双手又敏捷又急切——是个肤浅的丫头，只因休当过中学田径队队长，便以让人瞧见自己同他走在一起为荣。可她实在巴望他别这么不开窍。你瞧，他统共只吻过她一回，还只是傻乎乎地在她脸颊上轻轻碰了一下。兴许他只是害臊，可有的时候，她几乎可以肯定，他“简直就是个呆子”。

===Original===
They had walked silently along the country road to the woods that skirted the town. An early frost had already touched the foliage with scarlet and orange. They sat down on a fallen log, and Hugh gazed at a radiant maple-tree.

Helen let her hand drop lightly on his. “Thinking of me?” she asked softly.

Hugh squeezed her hand. “Yes,” he whispered, and looked at the ground while he scuffed some fallen leaves with the toe of his shoe.

===Chinese===
他们沿着乡间小路默默走着，一直走到环抱着镇子的树林边。早霜已经给树叶染上了猩红和橙黄。他们在一根倒下的树干上坐下，休凝望着一棵光彩夺目的枫树。

海伦把自己的手轻轻搭在他的手上。“在想我吗？”她柔声问。

休捏紧她的手。“是的。”他低声说，眼睛望着地面，一边用鞋尖拨弄着几片落叶。

===Original===
“I am going to miss you, Hughie﻿—oh, awfully. Are you going to miss me?”

He held her hand tightly and said nothing. He was aware only of her hand. His throat seemed to be stopped, choked with something.

A bird that should have been on its way south chirped from a tree near by. The sound made Hugh look up. He noticed that the shadows were lengthening. He and Helen would have to start back pretty soon or he would be late for dinner. There was still packing to do; his mother had said that his father wanted to have a talk with him﻿—and through all his thoughts there ran like a fiery red line the desire to kiss the girl whose hand was clasped in his.

He turned slightly toward her. “Hughie,” she whispered and moved close to him. His heart stopped as he loosened her hand from his and put his arm around her. With a contented sigh she rested her head on one shoulder and her hand on the other. “Hughie dear,” she breathed softly.

===Chinese===
“我会想你的，休吉（Hughie）——哦，想得不得了呢。你会想我吗？”

他紧紧攥着她的手，一言不发。他只意识到她的手。他的喉咙仿佛被什么堵住了，哽得慌。

一只本该已经动身南飞的鸟，在近旁的树上啁啾。这声音让休抬起头来。他发觉影子已经拉长了。他和海伦得赶紧往回走，不然他吃晚饭就要迟到了。行李还没有收拾；母亲说过，父亲要找他谈一谈——而在他全部的念头里，像一道火红的线贯穿始终的，是想去吻这个手正被他攥着的姑娘。

他微微朝她侧过身去。“休吉。”她低声唤着，朝他偎近。他把她的手从自己手里松开、伸手搂住她的时候，心仿佛都停止了跳动。她心满意足地轻叹一声，头枕在他一边肩上，手搭在另一边肩上。“亲爱的休吉。”她轻轻地呢喃。

===Original===
He hesitated no longer. His heart was beating so that he could not speak, but he bent and kissed her. And there they sat for half an hour more, close in each other’s embrace, speaking no words, but losing themselves in kisses that seemed to have no end.

Finally Hugh realized that darkness had fallen. He drew the yielding girl to her feet and started home, his arm around her. When they reached her gate, he embraced her once more and kissed her as if he could never let her go. A light flashed in a window. Frightened, he tried to leave, but she clung to him.

“I must go,” he whispered desperately.

“I’m going to miss you awfully.” He thought that she was weeping﻿—and kissed her again. Then as another window shot light into the yard, he forced her arms from around his neck.

===Chinese===
他不再迟疑。心跳得他连话都说不出来，但他俯下身去吻了她。两人就这样又坐了半个来钟头，紧紧相拥，不说一句话，只沉醉在一个个仿佛没有尽头的亲吻里。

最后，休终于发觉夜幕已经降临。他把柔顺的姑娘拉起身来，一条手臂搂着她动身往回走。到了她家院门口，他又一次拥抱她、吻她，仿佛永远也舍不得放她走。一扇窗户里灯光一闪。他着了慌，想要抽身，她却紧紧偎着他不放。

“我该走了。”他急得低声说。

“我会特别特别想你的。”他心想她在哭——便又吻了她一下。这时，又一扇窗户把灯光射进院子，他硬是把她环在自己脖子上的双臂扳开了。

===Original===
“Goodbye, Helen. Write to me.” His voice was rough and husky.

“Oh, I will. Goodbye﻿—darling.”

He walked home tingling with emotion. He wanted to shout; he felt suddenly grown up. Golly, but Helen was a little peach. He felt her arms around his neck again, her lips pressed maddeningly to his. For an instant he was dizzy.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
“再见，海伦。给我写信。”他的嗓音沙哑而低沉。

“哦，我会写的。再见——亲爱的。”

他心头发烫、浑身震颤地走回家去。他想放声大喊；他忽然觉得自己长大了。天哪，海伦可真是个小美人儿。他又觉出她的双臂环着自己的脖颈，她的嘴唇令人迷乱地贴在他的唇上。有一瞬间，他头晕目眩。……

---

===Original===
As he lay in bed in 19 Surrey thinking of Helen, he tried to summon that glorious intoxication again. But he failed. Carl, the college, registration﻿—a thousand thoughts intruded themselves. Already Helen seemed far away, a little nebulous. He wondered why.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
他躺在萨里楼（Surrey）19 室的床上想着海伦，想把那番辉煌的迷醉重新唤回来。可是他失败了。卡尔、大学、注册报到——千头万绪纷纷挤进脑海。海伦仿佛已经远了，变得有些朦胧。他不明白这是为什么。……

## III / 第三章

===Original===
For the next few days Carl and Hugh did little but wait in line. They lined up to register; they lined up to pay tuition; they lined up to shake hands with President Culver; they lined up to talk for two quite useless minutes with the freshman dean; they lined up to be assigned seats in the commons. Carl suggested that he and Hugh line up in the study before going to bed so that they would keep in practice. Then they had to attend lectures given by various members of the faculty about college customs, college manners, college honor, college everything. After the sixth of them, Hugh, thoroughly weary and utterly confused, asked Carl if he now had any idea of what college was.

“Yes,” replied Carl; “it’s a young ladies’ school for very nice boys.”

“Well,” Hugh said desperately, “if I have to listen to about two more awfully noble lectures, I’m going to get drunk. I have a hunch that college isn’t anything like what these old birds say it is. I hope not, anyway.”
===Chinese===
此后接连几天，卡尔和休除了排队，几乎什么也没干。他们排队注册；排队交学费；排队同卡尔弗校长（President Culver）握手；排队去同新生部主任谈上两分钟毫无用处的话；排队在公共食堂里分派座位。卡尔提议，他和休睡觉前先在书房里排一回队，免得把这门功夫荒疏了。随后，他们还不得不去听各位教员轮番登台的训导，讲大学的习俗、大学的礼数、大学的荣誉、大学的一切一切。听完第六场，休已经疲惫不堪、稀里糊涂，便问卡尔，这会儿他对大学到底是个什么名堂，心里有没有一点数了。

“是啊，”卡尔回答，“这是一所专收乖男孩的青年女子学校。”

“唉，”休绝望地说，“要是再听上两场这种高尚得不得了的训导，我就要去喝个烂醉。我有种预感，大学根本不是这些老家伙说的那个样子。反正，我希望不是。

===Original===
“Course it isn’t. Say, why wait for two more of the damn things to kill you off?” He pulled a flask out of his desk drawer and held it out invitingly.

Hugh laughed. “You told me yourself that that stuff was catgut and that you wouldn’t drink it on a bet. Besides, you know that I don’t drink. If I’m going to make my letter, I’ve got to keep in trim.”

“Right you are. Wish I knew what to do with this poison. If I leave it around here, the biddy’ll get hold of it, and then God help us. I’ll tell you what: after it gets dark tonight we’ll take it down and poison the waters of dear old Indian Lake.”

“All right. Say, I’ve got to pike along; I’ve got a date with my faculty adviser. Hope I don’t have to stand in line.”
===Chinese===
“当然不是。哎，何必再等两场那种该死的玩意儿来要你的命？”说着，他从书桌抽屉里掏出一只扁酒瓶，殷勤地递了过来。

休笑了。“你自个儿说过的，那玩意儿是猫尿一样的劣酒，打赌你也不肯喝。再说，你知道我不喝酒。我要是想挣得校队的字徽（letter），就得保持状态。”

“说得对。但愿我知道该拿这瓶毒药怎么办。要是随手搁在这儿，让那老妈子搜了去，到时候就只有求老天爷保佑了。我告诉你这么着吧：今晚天黑以后，咱们把它带下去，毒一毒亲爱的老印第安湖（Indian Lake）的湖水。”

“行。哎，我得开溜了；我跟导师约好了见面。希望不用再排队。”

===Original===
He didn’t have to stand in line﻿—he was permitted to sit﻿—but he did have to wait an hour and a half. Finally a student came out of the inner office, and a gruff voice from within called, “Next!”

“Just like a barber shop,” flashed across Hugh’s mind as he entered the tiny office.

An old-young man was sitting behind a desk shuffling papers. He glanced up as Hugh came in and motioned him to a chair beside him. Hugh sat down and stared at his feet.

“Um, let’s see. Your name’s﻿—what?”
===Chinese===
他倒不必站着排队——获准坐下了——可他足足等了一个半钟头。终于，有个学生从里间办公室走了出来，里面一个粗嘎的嗓门喊道：“下一位！”

“简直跟理发店一个样。”休走进那间小办公室时，这个念头在脑海里一闪而过。

一个年纪轻轻却满脸老气的人坐在办公桌后翻拣着纸片。休进来时，他抬眼扫了一下，抬手示意他坐到自己身旁的椅子上。休坐下了，两眼盯着自己的双脚。

“呃，我看看。你的名字是——什么来着？”

===Original===
“Carver, sir. Hugh Carver.”

The adviser, Professor Kane, glanced at some notes. “Oh, yes, from Merrytown High School, fully accredited. Are you taking an A.B. or a B.S.?”

“I﻿—I don’t know.”

“You have to have one year of college Latin for a B.S. and at least two years of Greek besides for an A.B.”
===Chinese===
“卡弗，先生。休·卡弗。”

这位导师凯恩教授（Professor Kane）扫了一眼几页记录。“哦，是的，梅里敦中学（Merrytown High School）毕业，资格完备。你是要读文学士（A.B.）还是理学士（B.S.）？”

“我——我不知道。”

“读理学士，得修一年大学拉丁文；读文学士，除此之外还得至少加修两年希腊文。”

===Original===
“Oh!” Hugh was frightened and confused. He knew that his father was an A.B., but he had heard the high-school principal say that Greek was useless nowadays. Suddenly he remembered: the principal had advised him to take a B.S.; he had said that it was more practical.

“I guess I’d better take a B.S.,” he said softly. “Very well.” Professor Kane, who hadn’t yet looked at Hugh, picked up a schedule card. “Any middle name?” he asked abruptly.

“Yes, sir﻿—Meredith.”

Kane scribbled H. M. Carver at the top of the card and then proceeded to fill it in rapidly. He hastily explained the symbols that he was using, but he did not say anything about the courses. When he had completed the schedule, he copied it on another card, handed one to Hugh, and stuck the other into a filing-box.
===Chinese===
“哦！”休又慌又乱。他知道父亲就是个文学士，可他听中学校长说过，如今希腊文一点用处也没有。他忽然想起来了：校长劝过他读理学士，说那更实用。

“我看我还是读理学士吧。”他小声说。“很好。”至今还没正眼瞧过休的凯恩教授拿起一张选课卡。“有中间名吗？”他没头没脑地问。

“有的，先生——梅雷迪思（Meredith）。”

凯恩在卡片顶端草草写下 H. M. Carver，随即飞快地填写起来。他用的那些符号都匆匆解释了一遍，可对各门课程本身却只字不提。填完课表，他又把它誊到另一张卡上，一张递给休，另一张插进一个档案盒里。
===Original===
“Anything else?” he asked, turning his blond, blank face toward Hugh for the first time.

Hugh stood up. There were a dozen questions that he wanted to ask. “No, sir,” he replied. “Very well, then. I am your regular adviser. You will come to me when you need assistance. Good day.”

“Good day, sir,” and as Hugh passed out of the door, the gruff voice bawled, “Next!” The boy nearest the door rose and entered the sanctum.

Hugh sought the open air and gazed at the hieroglyphics on the card. “Guess they mean something,” he mused, “but how am I going to find out?” A sudden fear made him blanch. “I bet I get into the wrong places. Oh, golly!”
===Chinese===
“还有别的事吗？”他问道，那张金发白净、茫无表情的脸这才头一回转向休。

休站起身来。他有一打问题想问。“没有了，先生。”他答道。“那好。我就是你的常任导师。需要帮助的时候就来找我了。再见。”

“再见，先生。”休走出房门时，那粗嘎的嗓门又吼了起来：“下一位！”离门最近的那个男孩起身，走进了那间“圣室”。

休走到露天里，盯着卡片上那些天书般的符号出神。“想来它们总有番意思，”他暗自琢磨，“可叫我怎么弄明白呢？”一阵突如其来的恐惧使他脸色发白。“我保准会走错地方。哦，天哪！”

---

===Original===
Then came the upper-classmen, nearly seven hundred of them. The quiet campus became a bedlam of excitement and greetings. “Hi, Jack. Didya have a good summer?”﻿ ﻿… “Well, Tom, ol’ kid, I sure am glad to see you back.”﻿ ﻿… “Put her there, ol’ scout; it’s sure good to see you.” Everywhere the same greetings: “Didya have a good summer? Glad to see you back.” Everyone called everyone else by his first name; everyone shook hands with astonishing vigor, usually clutching the other fellow by the forearm at the same time. How cockily these lads went around the campus! No confusion or fear for them; they knew what to do.

For the first time Hugh felt a pang of homesickness; for the first time he realized that he wasn’t yet part of the college. He clung close to Carl and one or two other lads in Surrey with whom he picked up an acquaintance, and Carl clung close to Hugh, careful to hide the fact that he felt very small and meek. For the first time *he* realized that he was just a freshman﻿—and he didn’t like it.

Then suddenly the tension, which had been gathering for a day or so, broke. Orders went out from the upper-classmen that all freshmen put on their baby bonnets, silly little blue caps with a bright orange button. From that moment every freshman was doomed. Work was their lot, and plenty of it. “Hi, freshman, carry up my trunk. Yeah, you, freshman﻿—you with the skinny legs. You and your fat friend carry my trunk up to the fourth floor﻿—and if you drop it, I’ll break your fool necks.”﻿ ﻿… “Freshman! go down to the station and get my suitcases. Here are the checks. Hurry back if you know what’s good for you.”﻿ ﻿… “Freshman! go up to Hill Twenty-eight and put the beds together.”﻿ ﻿… “Freshman! come up to my room. I want you to hang pictures.”
===Chinese===
接着来的是高年级学生，足有将近七百人。安静的校园顿时变成了一座喧腾着激动与寒暄的疯人院。“嗨，杰克。夏天过得好吗？”……“哎呀，汤姆，老伙计，看见你回来我真是高兴。”……“来，把手搁这儿，老哥们儿；见到你可真好。”到处都是千篇一律的问候：“夏天过得好吗？真高兴你又回来了。”人人彼此直呼教名；人人握手，握得出奇地使劲，通常还一面攥住对方的小臂。这些小伙子在校园里大摇大摆地转来转去，好不神气！他们没有半点慌乱，没有半点畏惧；他们知道该干什么。

休生平第一次感到一阵想家的刺痛；也是生平第一次，他意识到自己还不算这所大学的一员。他紧紧贴着卡尔，还有萨里楼里一两个刚攀上话的男孩；卡尔也紧紧贴着休，小心翼翼地掩藏着心里那份自觉渺小与怯懦。生平第一次，*他*也意识到自己不过是个一年级新生——而他不喜欢这样。

后来，绷了约莫一天的那股紧张气氛骤然炸开了。高年级学生传下号令：所有新生一律戴上娃娃帽——一种傻乎乎的小蓝帽，缀着一颗鲜亮的橙色纽扣。从那一刻起，每个新生都在劫难逃。干活就是他们分内的事，而且有的是活儿。“嗨，新生，把我的箱子搬上去。对，就是说你，新生——说你呢，细腿的那个。你，还有你那个胖子朋友，把我的箱子搬到四楼去——要是摔了，我就拧断你们俩的蠢脖子。”……“新生！到车站去把我的手提箱取来。喏，取行李的牌子给你。识相的话就快去快回。”……“新生！上希尔楼二十八号去，把床拼搭起来。”……“新生！到我房间来一趟，我要你挂画。”

===Original===
Fortunately the labor did not last long, but while it lasted Hugh was hustled around as he never had been before. And he loved it. He loved his blue cap and its orange button; he loved the upper-classmen who called him freshman and ordered him around; he loved the very trunks that he lugged so painfully upstairs. He was being recognized, merely as a janitor, it is true, but recognized; at last he was a part of Sanford College. Further, one of the men who had ordered him around the most fiercely wore a Nu Delta pin, the emblem of his father’s fraternity. He ran that man’s errands with such speed and willingness that the hero decided that the freshman was “very, very dumb.”

That night Hugh and Carl sat in 19 Surrey and rested their aching bones, one on a couch, the other in a leather Morris chair.

“Hot stuff, wasn’t it?” said Hugh, stretching out comfortably.
===Chinese===
所幸这番苦役没有持续多久，可只要还在干，休就被人支使得团团转，从来没有这样被人支使过。而他乐在其中。他爱他那顶缀着橙色纽扣的小蓝帽；他爱那些管他叫新生、把他差来遣去的高年级学生；他连那些害他辛辛苦苦搬上楼的箱子都爱。他得到了承认——不错，只是被当成一个看门打杂的小工，可到底是得到了承认；他终于成了桑福德学院（Sanford College）的一部分。再说，差遣他差遣得最凶的一个人，襟上正别着一枚“纽德尔塔”（Nu Delta）徽章——那是他父亲当年兄弟会的标记。他替那人跑腿，跑得那么飞快、那么心甘情愿，那位英雄人物于是断定，这个新生“蠢得很，非常蠢”。

那天夜里，休和卡尔坐在萨里楼 19 室歇着酸痛的骨头，一个躺在睡椅上，一个坐在皮面的莫里斯躺椅（Morris chair）里。

“够带劲的，是不是？”休说着，舒舒服服地舒展开身子。

===Original===
“Hot stuff, hell! How do they get that way?”

“Never mind; we’ll do the ordering next year.”

“Right you are,” said Carl decisively, lighting a cigarette, “and won’t I make the little frosh walk.” He gazed around the room, his face beaming with satisfaction. “Say, we’re pretty snappy here, aren’t we?”

Hugh, too, looked around admiringly. The walls were almost hidden by banners, a huge Sanford blanket﻿—Hugh’s greatest contribution﻿—Carl’s Kane blanket, the photographs of the “harem,” posters of college athletes and movie bathing-girls, pipe-racks, and three Maxfield Parrish prints.

“It certainly is fine,” said Hugh proudly. “All we need is a barber pole and a street sign.”

“We’ll have ’em before the week is out.” This with great decision.
===Chinese===
“去他的带劲！他们凭什么摆这副架子？”

“别计较；明年就轮到咱们发号施令了。”

“说得对，”卡尔果断地说着，点上一支烟，“到时候瞧我不支得那帮小新生团团转。”他环视着房间，脸上放出满意的光彩。“嘿，咱们这地方够气派的，是不是？”

休也满怀欣赏地四下打量。墙壁几乎都被遮满了：一面面旗帜，一床大大的桑福德毛毯——这是休最得力的贡献——卡尔那床凯恩中学的毯子，“后宫”佳丽的照片，大学运动员和电影泳装女郎的海报，几只烟斗架，还有三幅马克斯菲尔德·帕里什（Maxfield Parrish）的印画。

“这儿可真不赖，”休自豪地说，“咱们就差一根理发店的转花柱和一块街名牌了。”

“不出这个礼拜，咱们就能把它们弄到手。”这话他说得斩钉截铁。

## IV / 第四章

===Original===
Carl’s adviser had been less efficient than Hugh’s; therefore he knew what his courses were, where the classes met and the hours, the names of his instructors, and the requirements other than Latin for a B.S. degree. Carl said that he was taking a B.S. because he had had a year of Greek at Kane and was therefore perfectly competent to make full use of the language; he could read the letters on the front doors of the fraternity houses.

The boys found that their courses were the same but that they were in different sections. Hugh was in a dilemma; he could make nothing out of his card.

“Here,” said Carl, “give the thing to me. My adviser was a good scout and wised me up. This P.C. isn’t paper cutting as you might suppose; it’s gym. You’ll get out of that by signing up for track. P.C. means physical culture. Think of that! You can sign up for track any time tomorrow down at the gym. And E I, 7 means that you’re in English I, Section 7; and M is math. You re in Section 3. Lat means Latin, of course﻿—Section 6. My adviser﻿—he tried pretty hard to be funny﻿—said that G.S. wasn’t glorious salvation but general science. That meets in the big lecture hall in Cranston. We all go to that. And H I, 4 means that you are in Section 4 of History I. See? That’s all there is to it. Now this thing”﻿—he held up a printed schedule﻿—“tells you where the classes meet.”

With a great deal of labor, discussion, and profanity they finally got a schedule made out that meant something to Hugh. He heaved a Brobdingnagian sigh of relief when they finished.

===Chinese===
卡尔的导师不像休的导师那样办事麻利；因此，他把自己修的课程、上课的地点和钟点、授课老师的姓名，还有读理学士（B.S.）学位除拉丁文之外的要求，全都弄得一清二楚。卡尔说，他之所以读理学士，是因为他在凯恩中学学过一年希腊文，因而完全有本事让这门语言物尽其用——兄弟会宅子大门上的那些字母，他都念得出来。

两人发现彼此的课程一模一样，只是分在不同的班。休犯了难；那张卡片他怎么看也看不明白。

“来，”卡尔说，“把这玩意儿给我。我的导师人很够意思，把底细都指点给我了。这个 P.C.，可不是你想的‘剪纸’（paper cutting），是体育课。你只要报上田径，就能免掉它。P.C. 的意思是体能训练（physical culture）。你想想！明天什么时候都行，到体育馆去把田径报上。还有，E I, 7 的意思是，你上英语一（English I）的第七班；M 是数学，你在第三班。Lat 当然是拉丁文——第六班。我的导师——他挺卖力地想显得风趣——说 G.S. 不是‘光荣的救赎’（glorious salvation），而是普通科学（general science）。那门课在克兰斯顿楼（Cranston）的大阶梯教室上，咱们全都去上。还有 H I, 4，意思是你在历史一（History I）的第四班。明白了吧？就这么点儿事。喏，这个东西”——他举起一张印好的课表——“告诉你各门课都在哪儿上。”

费了老大的劲，又是商量又是骂骂咧咧，两人总算排出了一张休能看明白的课表。完事的时候，他如释重负，发出了一声大人国巨人般的长叹。

===Original===
“Well,” he exclaimed, “that’s that! At last I know where I’m going. You certainly saved my life. I know where all the buildings are; so it ought to be easy.”

“Sure,” said Carl encouragingly; “it’s easy. Now there’s nothing to do till tomorrow until eight forty-five when we attend chapel to the glory of the Lord. I think I’ll pray tomorrow; I may need it. Christ! I hate to study.”

“Me, too,” Hugh lied. He really loved books, but somehow he couldn’t admit the fact, which had suddenly become shameful, to Carl. “Let’s go to the movies,” he suggested, changing the subject for safety.

“Right-o!” Carl put on his freshman cap and flung Hugh’s to him. “Gloria Nielsen is there, and she’s a pash baby. Ought to be a good fillum.”

===Chinese===
“嘿，”他欢呼道，“搞定啦！总算知道该往哪儿去了。你真是救了我一命。各栋楼在哪儿我都知道，所以应该不难。”

“当然，”卡尔鼓劲道，“不难。眼下一直闲到明天八点四十五，咱们去礼拜堂做礼拜，荣耀主的名。我想明天我要祷告一番，说不定用得着。上帝啊！我恨念书。”

“我也恨，”休撒了个谎。他其实真爱读书，可是不知怎么，这个事实他怎么也没法对卡尔承认——它一下子变成桩丢人的事了。“咱们看电影去吧，”他提议道，为保险起见岔开了话题。

“好——嘞！”卡尔戴上自己的新生帽，把休的那顶抛给他。“格洛丽亚·尼尔森（Gloria Nielsen）在里头，她可是个勾魂的小宝贝。准是部好片子。”

===Original===
The Blue and Orange﻿—it was the only movie theater in town﻿—was almost full when the boys arrived. Only a few seats near the front were still vacant. A freshman started down the aisle, his “baby bonnet” stuck jauntily on the back of his head.

“Freshman!”﻿ ﻿… “Kill him!”﻿ ﻿… “Murder the frosh!” Shouts came from all parts of the house, and an instant later hundreds of peanuts shot swiftly at the startled freshman. “Cap! Cap! Cap off!” There was a panic of excitement. Upper-classmen were standing on their chairs to get free throwing room. The freshman snatched off his cap, drew his head like a scared turtle down into his coat collar, and ran for a seat. Hugh and Carl tucked their caps into their coat pockets and attempted to stroll nonchalantly down the aisle. They hadn’t taken three steps before the bombardment began. Like their classmate, they ran for safety.

Then someone in the front of the theatre threw a peanut at someone in the rear. The fight was on! Yelling like madmen, the students stood on their chairs and hurled peanuts, the front and rear of the house automatically dividing into enemy camps. When the fight was at its hottest, three girls entered.

“Wimmen! Wimmen!” As the girls walked down the aisle, infinitely pleased with their reception, five hundred men stamped in time with their steps.

===Chinese===
“蓝与橙”（The Blue and Orange）——全镇唯一的一家电影院——两个小伙子到的时候，里面几乎已经座无虚席。只有前排附近还空着几个位子。一个新生顺着过道往里走，那顶“娃娃帽”神气活现地扣在后脑勺上。

“新生！”……“宰了他！”……“弄死这新生崽子！”喊声从场子四面八方炸起，转眼间，几百颗花生米呼啸着飞向那个惊慌失措的新生。“摘帽！摘帽！快摘帽！”全场兴奋得乱作一团。高年级学生纷纷站到椅子上去，好腾出投掷的空间。那新生一把扯下帽子，脑袋像只受了惊的乌龟似的缩进大衣领里，撒腿奔向一个座位。休和卡尔把帽子塞进大衣口袋，装出一副满不在乎的样子，想悠悠然踱下过道去。没走出三步，弹雨就劈头盖脸地砸了下来。两人跟他们那位同学一样，飞奔逃命去了。

接着，剧场前排有人朝后排扔了一颗花生。大战开锣了！学生们像疯子似的叫喊着，站到椅子上猛掷花生，场子的前排和后排自动分成了敌对的营垒。战事正酣之际，三个姑娘走了进来。

“娘们儿！娘们儿！”姑娘们顺着过道往里走，对这样的欢迎大为受用，五百个男人合着她们的步点，齐声跺脚。

===Original===
No sooner were the girls seated than there was a scramble in one corner, an excited scuffling of feet. “I’ve got it!” a boy screamed. He stood on his chair and held up a live mouse by its tail. There was a shout of applause and then﻿—“Play catch!”

The boy dropped the writhing mouse into a peanut bag, screwed the open end tight-closed, and then threw the bag far across the room. Another boy caught it and threw it, this time over the girls’ heads. They screamed and jumped upon their chairs, holding their skirts, and dancing up and down in assumed terror. Back over their heads, back and over, again and again the bagged mouse was thrown while the girls screamed and the boys roared with delight. Suddenly one of the girls threw up her arm, caught the bag deftly, held it for a second, and then tossed it into the rear of the theater.

Cheers of terrifying violence broke loose: “Ray! Ray! Atta girl! Hot dog! Ray, ray!” And then the lights went out.

“Moosick! Moosick! Moo-*sick*!” The audience stamped and roared, whistled and howled. “Moosick! We want moosick!”

===Chinese===
姑娘们刚一落座，一个角落里就闹腾起来，一阵兴奋的脚步乱蹬。“逮着了！”一个男孩尖叫道。他站到椅子上，拎着尾巴举起一只活老鼠。全场爆出一片喝彩，接着——“传来传去玩！”

那男孩把扭动挣扎的老鼠丢进一只装花生的纸袋，把敞开的袋口拧得紧紧的，然后把袋子远远扔到场子那头。另一个男孩接住，又扔了出去，这一回是打姑娘们的头顶上飞过去。她们尖叫着跳上椅子，揪着裙子，装出吓坏了的样子又蹦又跳。装在袋子里的老鼠从她们头顶上飞过去，又飞回来，再飞过去，一次又一次，姑娘们尖声叫个不停，小伙子们乐得吼声震天。冷不丁地，其中一个姑娘扬起手臂，利落地一把接住袋子，攥了一秒钟，随即把它甩到了剧场后排。

一阵惊天动地的喝彩炸了开来：“蕾！蕾！好样的，姑娘！好家伙！蕾，蕾！”接着，灯灭了。

“哞西克！哞西克！哞—*西克*！”观众又跺脚又吼叫，又是吹口哨又是嚎。“哞西克！我们要哞西克！”

===Original===
The pianist, an undergraduate, calmly strolled down the aisle.

“Get a move on!”﻿ ﻿… “Earn your salary!”﻿ ﻿… “Give us moosick!”

The pianist paused to thumb his nose casually at the entire audience, and then amid shouts and hisses sat down at the piano and began to play “Love Nest.”

Immediately the boys began to whistle, and as the comedy was utterly stupid, they relieved their boredom by whistling the various tunes that the pianist played until the miserable film flickered out.

===Chinese===
那位钢琴师是个本校本科生，他不慌不忙地顺着过道踱了下去。

“快着点儿！”……“挣你的薪水去！”……“给我们哞西克！”

钢琴师停住脚步，好整以暇地朝全体观众做了个抹鼻子的手势，然后在叫喊声与嘘声之中坐到钢琴前，弹起了《爱巢》（Love Nest）。

男孩们随即吹起了口哨；那部喜剧片蠢得无可救药，他们便吹着钢琴师弹出的一支又一支曲子解闷，直到那部寒碜的影片忽明忽暗地放完。

===Original===
Then the “feature” and the fun began. During the stretches of pure narrative, the boys whistled, but when there was any real action they talked. The picture was a melodrama of “love and hate,” as the advertisement said.

The boys told the actors what to do; they revealed to them the secrets of the plot. “She’s hiding behind the door, Harold. No, no! Not that way. Hey, dumbbell﻿—behind the door.”﻿ ﻿… “Catch him, Gloria; he’s only shy!”﻿ ﻿… “No, that’s not him!”

The climactic fight brought shouts of encouragement﻿—to the villain. “Kill him!”﻿ ﻿… “Shoot one to his kidneys!”﻿ ﻿… “Ahhhhh,” as the villain hit the hero in the stomach.﻿ ﻿… “Muss his hair. Attaboy!”﻿ ﻿… “Kill the skunk!” And finally groans of despair when the hero won his inevitable victory.

But it was the love scenes that aroused the greatest ardor and joy. The hero was given careful instructions. “Some neckin’, Harold!”﻿ ﻿… “Kiss her! Kiss her! Ahhh!”﻿ ﻿… “Harold, Harold, you’re getting rough!”﻿ ﻿… “She’s vamping you, Harold!”﻿ ﻿… “Stop it; Gloria; he’s a good boy.” And so on until the picture ended in the usual closeup of the hero and heroine silhouetted in a tender embrace against the setting sun. The boys breathed “Ahhhh” and “Ooooh” ecstatically﻿—and laughed. The meretricious melodrama did not fool them, but they delighted in its absurdities.

===Chinese===
接着，“正片”和真正的乐趣一起开场了。每逢纯粹叙事的冗长段落，男孩们就吹口哨；一到真有动静的地方，他们就聊起天来。正像广告所说，这部片子是出一台“爱与恨”的情节剧。

男孩们指点演员们该怎么演，把情节的秘密一股脑儿透露给他们。“她躲在门后头呢，哈罗德（Harold）。不，不！不是那么回事。嘿，笨蛋——门后头。”……“逮住他，格洛丽亚；他不过是害臊！”……“不对，那人不是他！”

高潮的打斗引来一片助威的呐喊——是给反派助威。“宰了他！”……“照他腰眼上狠狠来一记！”……“啊————”，这是反派一拳打中英雄肚子的时候。……“把他的头发揉乱。好小子！”……“宰了那只臭鼬！”末了，英雄不出所料地大获全胜，全场发出一片绝望的哀嚎。

可是，最能点起炽烈兴头和欢喜的，还是那些谈情说爱的场面。英雄得到了谆谆指教。“搂得够味儿，哈罗德！”……“亲她！亲她！啊——！”……“哈罗德，哈罗德，你下手粗啦！”……“她在勾你的魂儿呢，哈罗德！”……“住手，格洛丽亚；他是个乖孩子。”如此这般，一直演到影片照例收场：男女主角柔情相拥的剪影，衬着一轮落日。男孩们心醉神迷地呼出“啊——”“噢——”——随即大笑起来。这俗不可耐的情节剧骗不了他们，可他们对它的荒唐之处正自乐在其中。

===Original===
The lights flashed on and the crowd filed out, “wisecracking” about the picture and commenting favorably on the heroine’s figure. There were shouts to this fellow or that fellow to come on over and play bridge, and suggestions here and there to go to a drug store and get a drink.

Hugh and Carl strolled home over the dark campus, both of them radiant with excitement, Hugh frankly so.

“Golly, I did enjoy that,” he exclaimed. “I never had a better time. It was sure hot stuff. I don’t want to go to the room; let’s walk for a while.”

“Yeah, it was pretty good,” Carl admitted. “Nope, I can’t go walking; gotta write a letter.”

===Chinese===
灯光刷地亮起，人群鱼贯而出，一面对影片“插科打诨”，一面称许着女主角的身段。有人喊着叫这个那个家伙过来打桥牌，这儿那儿还有人提议去药房喝上一杯。

休和卡尔穿过黑黢黢的校园踱回宿舍，两人都兴奋得满面红光——休更是毫不掩饰。

“天哪，我真是痛快透了，”他欢呼道。“从来没这么痛快过。的确够带劲。我不想回房间去；咱们再走一会儿吧。”

“是啊，挺不赖的，”卡尔承认道。“不行，我不能去遛弯儿；我得写封信。”

===Original===
“Who to? The harem?”

Carl hunched his shoulders until his ears touched his coat collar. “Gettin’ cold. Fall’s here. Nope, not the harem. My old lady.”

Hugh looked at him bewildered. He was finding Carl more and more a conundrum. He consistently called his mother his old lady, insisted that she was a damned nuisance﻿—and wrote to her every night. Hugh was writing to his mother only twice a week. It was very confusing.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
“写给谁呀？‘后宫’佳丽？”

卡尔耸起肩膀，耸得耳朵都碰到了大衣领子。“天冷起来了。秋天到了。不是，不是‘后宫’佳丽。是我家老太太。”

休一头雾水地望着他。他越发觉得卡尔像一道解不开的谜。这家伙一口一个“老太太”称呼自己的母亲，咬定她烦死人了——却每天晚上都给她写信。休给自己母亲写信，一星期不过两封。真是叫人犯糊涂。……

## IX / 第九章

===Original===
Hugh was by no means continuously depressed; as a matter of fact, most of the time he was agog with delight, especially over the rallies that were occurring with increasing frequency as the football season progressed. Sometimes the rallies were carefully prepared ceremonies held in the gymnasium; sometimes they were entirely spontaneous.

===Chinese===
休的心情绝没有一直低落下去；说实在的，他大部分时间都欢喜得心痒难耐，尤其是对那一场场助威大会（rallies）——随着橄榄球赛季的推进，它们开得一次比一次勤。这些大会有时是在体育馆里精心筹备的仪式，有时又完全是自发的。

===Original===
A group of men would rush out of a dormitory or fraternity house yelling, “Peerade, peerade!” Instantly everyone within hearing would drop his books﻿—or his cards﻿—and rush to the yelling group, which would line up in fours and begin circling the campus, the line ever getting longer as more men came running out of the dormitories and fraternity houses. On, on they would go, arm in arm, dancing, singing Sanford songs, past every dormitory on the campus, past every fraternity house﻿—pausing occasionally to give a cheer, always, however, keeping one goal in mind, the fraternity house where the team lived during the football season. Then when the cheerleaders and the team were heading the procession, the mob would make for the football field, with the cry of “Wood, freshmen, wood!” ringing down the line.

===Chinese===
常常会有那么一群人，从哪栋宿舍楼或兄弟会宅子里冲出来，高喊着“游行喽！游行喽！”顷刻之间，凡是听得见的人都会丢下手里的书——或者牌——朝喊声奔去；那群人便四人一排排好队伍，开始绕着校园行进，随着宿舍楼和兄弟会宅子里不断有人跑出来，队伍越拉越长。他们就这么走下去，走下去，臂挽着臂，边舞边唱着桑福德的歌，走过校园里的每一栋宿舍楼，走过每一座兄弟会宅子——时不时停下来喊上一通欢呼，心里却始终惦着同一个目标，也就是橄榄球赛季期间球队住的那座兄弟会宅子。等到啦啦队长们和球队领头走在队伍前头，人群便朝橄榄球场进发，“柴火，新生们，柴火！”的喊声顺着队伍一路传下去。

===Original===
Hugh was always one of the first freshmen to break from the line in his eagerness to get wood. In an incredibly short time he and his classmates had found a large quantity of old lumber, empty boxes, rotten planks, and not very rotten gates. When a light was applied to the clumsy pile of wood, the flames leaped up quickly﻿—someone always seemed to have a supply of kerosene ready﻿—and revealed the excited upper-classmen sitting on the bleachers.

===Chinese===
抢柴火的时候，休总是最先脱队的几个新生之一。在短得叫人难以置信的时间里，他和同学们就搜罗来一大堆旧木料、空箱子、烂木板，还有不算太烂的栅栏门。火苗一触到那堆笨重的柴垛，火头便腾地蹿起——总好像有谁早就备下了一桶煤油——照出看台上坐得兴奋不已的高年级学生。

===Original===
“Dance, freshmen, dance!”

===Chinese===
“跳起来，新生们，跳起来！”

===Original===
Then the freshmen danced around the fire, holding hands and spreading into an ever widening circle as the fire crackled and the flames leaped upward. Slowly, almost impressively, the upper-classmen chanted:

===Chinese===
于是新生们手拉着手，围着火堆跳起舞来；柴火噼啪作响，火苗向上蹿腾，他们的圈子越扩越大。高年级学生缓缓地、近乎肃穆地吟唱起来：

===Original===
> “Round the fire, the freshmen go,
 

 Freshmen go,
 

 Freshmen go;
 

 Round the fire the freshmen go
 

 To cheer Sanford.”

===Chinese===
> “绕着篝火，新生走，
 

 新生走，
 

 新生走；
 

 绕着篝火新生走
 

 为桑福德欢呼。”

===Original===
The song had a dozen stanzas, only the last line of each being different. The freshmen danced until the last verse was sung, which ended with the Sanford cheer:

===Chinese===
这首歌共有十二段，每段只有末一行不同。新生们一直跳到最后一遍唱完，那一段以桑福德的欢呼收尾：

===Original===
> “Closer now the freshmen go,
 

 Freshmen go,
 

 Freshmen go;
 

 Closer now the freshmen go
 

 To cheer﻿—

> *Sanford!*
 
 

 Sanford! Rah, rah!
 

 Sanford! Sanford!
 

 San﻿—San﻿—San﻿—
 

 San﻿—ford, San﻿—ford﻿—San﻿—*ford*!”

===Chinese===
> “越凑越近，新生走，
 

 新生走，
 

 新生走；
 

 越凑越近新生走
 

 来欢呼——

> *桑福德！*
 
 

 桑福德！啦，啦！
 

 桑福德！桑福德！
 

 桑——桑——桑——
 

 桑——福德，桑——福德——桑——*福德*！”

===Original===
While the upper-classmen were singing the last stanza the freshmen slowly closed in on the dying fire. At the first word of the cheer, they stopped, turned toward the grand stand, and joined the cheering. That over, they broke and ran for the bleachers, scrambling up the wooden stands, shoving each other out of the way, laughing and shouting.

===Chinese===
高年级学生唱最后一段的时候，新生们朝着渐渐熄灭的火堆慢慢围拢过去。欢呼的第一个字一出口，他们便停下脚步，转向大看台，跟着喝彩起来。那一通完了，他们一哄而散，朝看台奔去，手脚并用地爬上木头看台，你推我搡，又笑又喊。

===Original===
The football captain usually made a short and very awkward speech, which was madly applauded; perhaps the coach said a few words; two or three cheers were given; and finally everyone rose, took off his hat if he wore one﻿—nearly everyone but the freshmen went bareheaded﻿—and sang the college hymn, simply and religiously. Then the crowd broke, straggling in groups across the campus, chatting, singing, shouting to each other. Suddenly lights began to flash in the dormitory windows. In less than an hour after the first cry of “Peerade!” the men were back in their rooms, once more studying, talking, or playing cards.

===Chinese===
橄榄球队队长照例来一篇简短而十分笨拙的讲话，博得疯狂的掌声；教练或许也说上几句；再喊上两三通欢呼；最后全体起立，戴帽子的——除了新生，几乎人人光着脑袋——把帽子摘下，朴素而虔敬地唱起学院圣歌。随后人群散开，三三两两、零零落落地穿过校园，一路聊天、唱歌、彼此喊话。蓦然间，宿舍楼的窗户里灯光一盏接一盏亮了起来。从第一声“游行喽！”喊响算起，不出一个钟头，大家已经回到各自的房里，重新念书的念书，聊天的聊天，打牌的打牌。

===Original===
It was the smoker rallies, though, that Hugh found the most thrilling, especially the last one before the final game of the season, the “big game” with Raleigh College. There were 1123 students in Sanford, and more than 1000 were at the rally. A rough platform had been built at one end of the gymnasium. On one side of it sat the band, on the other side the Glee Club﻿—and before it the mass of students, smoking cigarettes, corncob pipes, and, occasionally, a cigar. The “smokes” had been furnished free by a local tobacconist; so everybody smoked violently and too much. In half an hour it was almost impossible to see the ceiling through the dull blue haze, and the men in the rear of the gymnasium saw the speakers on the platform dimly through a wavering mist.

===Chinese===
不过，最让休觉得惊心动魄的，还是“烟会”助威大会（smoker rallies），尤其是赛季最后一场比赛——同罗利学院（Raleigh College）的“大赛”——之前的那最后一次。桑福德共有 1123 名学生，到会的超过 1000 人。体育馆的一端搭起了一座粗陋的主席台。台的一侧坐着乐队，另一侧坐着男声合唱团（Glee Club）——台前则是黑压压的一大片学生，抽着香烟、玉米芯烟斗，偶尔还有人抽一支雪茄。“烟”是镇上一家烟商免费供应的；于是人人抽得起劲，抽过了量。不到半个钟头，暗蓝色的烟霭已浓得几乎望不见天花板，体育馆后排的人看台上的演讲者，得透过一层晃动的烟雾，只见影影绰绰的影子。

===Original===
The band played various Sanford songs, and everybody sang. Occasionally Wayne Gifford, the cheerleader, leaped upon the platform, raised a megaphone to his mouth, and shouted, “A regular cheer for Sanford﻿—a regular cheer for Sanford.” Then he lifted his arms above his head, flinging the megaphone aside with the same motion, and waited tense and rigid until the students were on their feet. Suddenly he turned into a mad dervish, twisting, bending, gesticulating, leaping, running back and forth across the platform, shouting, and finally throwing his hands above his head and springing high into the air at the concluding “San﻿—*ford*!”

===Chinese===
乐队奏起一支又一支桑福德的歌，人人放声齐唱。啦啦队长韦恩·吉福德（Wayne Gifford）时不时跃上主席台，举起扩音喇叭喊道：“给桑福德来一支正彩——给桑福德来一支正彩。”随即双臂举过头顶，顺势把喇叭甩向一旁，绷紧了身子直挺挺地候着，直到学生们全体起立。刹那间，他化作一个疯魔的旋转托钵僧，拧腰、屈身、比画、腾跃，在台上来回疾走，高声呐喊，末了随着收尾那声“桑——*福德*！”双手抛过头顶，高高蹦起。

===Original===
The Glee Club sang to mad applause; a tenor twanged a ukulele and moaned various blues; a popular professor told stories, some of them funny, most of them slightly off color; a former cheerleader told of the triumphs of former Sanford teams﻿—and the atmosphere grew denser and denser, bluer and bluer, as the smoke wreathed upward. The thousand boys leaned intently forward, occasionally jumping to their feet to shout and cheer, and then sinking back into their chairs, tense and excited. As each speaker mounted the platform they shouted: “Off with your coat! Off with your coat!” And the speakers, even the professor, had to shed their coats before they were permitted to say a word.

===Chinese===
男声合唱团的演唱博得狂热的掌声；一位男高音拨响尤克里里，呜呜咽咽地唱了几段布鲁斯；一位广得人心的教授讲了几个故事，有的好笑，多数稍稍带了点颜色；一位前任啦啦队长历数历届桑福德球队的赫赫战功——烟雾袅袅上升，空气越来越浓，越来越蓝。上千个男孩全神贯注地前倾着身子，不时跳起来喊叫喝彩，随即又跌回椅中，绷得紧紧的，亢奋不已。每逢有人登台演讲，他们便齐声大喊：“脱掉外衣！脱掉外衣！”于是演讲的人，连教授也在内，都得先把外衣脱了，才被允许开口说一个字。

===Original===
When the team entered, bedlam broke loose. Every student stood on his chair, waved his arms, slapped his neighbor on the back or hugged him wildly, threw his hat in the air, if he had one﻿—and, so great was his training, keeping an eye on the cheerleader, who was on the platform going through a series of indescribable contortions. Suddenly he straightened up, held his hands above his head again, and shouted through his megaphone: “A regular cheer for the team﻿—a regular cheer for the team. Make it big﻿—*big*! Ready﻿—!” Away whirled the megaphone, and he went through exactly the same performance that he had used before in conducting the regular cheer. Gifford looked like an inspired madman, but he knew exactly what he was doing. The students cheered lustily, so lustily that some of them were hoarse the next day. They continued to yell after the cheer was completed, ceasing only when Gifford signaled for silence.

===Chinese===
球队一进场，全场顿时闹翻了天。每个学生都站到椅子上，挥舞手臂，拍邻座的背，或是忘情地搂住他，把帽子抛向空中——如果头上有帽子的话——同时，到底是训练有素，谁都不忘盯住台上的啦啦队长，他正在那里做出一连串难以言状的扭曲动作。突然，他直起身来，重新把双手举过头顶，对着喇叭喊道：“给球队来一支正彩——给球队来一支正彩。喊响亮些——*响亮*！预备——！”喇叭嗖地飞开，他又分毫不差地把方才指挥正彩的那一套从头演了一遍。吉福德看上去像个神灵附体的疯子，可他对自己在做什么知道得一清二楚。学生们纵情欢呼，卖力到第二天有些人嗓子都哑了。欢呼结束后喊声仍不停歇，直到吉福德示意肃静，才渐渐止住。

===Original===
Then there were speeches by each member of the team, all enthusiastically applauded, and finally the speech of the evening, that of the coach, Jack Price. He was a big, compactly built man with regular features, heavy blond hair, and pale, cold blue eyes. He threw off his coat with a belligerent gesture, stuck his hands into his trousers pockets, and waited rigidly until the cheering had subsided. Then he began:

===Chinese===
接着，球队的每名队员都讲了话，个个博得热烈的掌声；最后是当晚压轴的讲话——教练杰克·普赖斯（Jack Price）的演讲。他生得高大敦实，五官端正，一头浓密的金发，一双淡蓝色的冷眼睛。他带着挑衅的姿态甩掉外衣，双手插进裤袋，直挺挺地立着，直等到欢呼平息下去才开口：

===Original===
“Go ahead and yell. It’s easy as hell to cheer here in the gym; but what are you going to do Saturday afternoon?”

===Chinese===
“喊吧，尽管喊。在体育馆里喊两嗓子，容易得要死；可到星期六下午，你们打算干什么？”

===Original===
His voice was sharp with sarcasm, and to the shouts of “Yell! Fight!” that came from all over the gymnasium, he answered, “Yeah, maybe﻿—maybe.” He shifted his position, stepping toward the front of the platform, thrusting his hands deeper into his pockets.

===Chinese===
他的嗓音尖利，满是讥诮；体育馆四面八方涌来“喊呀！拼呀！”的喊声，他只应了一句：“是啊，也许——也许。”他换了个姿势，朝台前跨出一步，双手往裤袋里插得更深。

===Original===
“I’ve seen a lot of football games, and I’ve seen lots of rooters, but this is the goddamndest gang of yellow-bellied quitters that I’ve ever seen. What happened last Saturday when we were behind? I’m asking you; what happened? You quit! Quit like a bunch of whipped curs. God! you’re yellow, yellow as hell. But the team went on fighting﻿—and it won, won in spite of you, won for a bunch of yellow pups. And why? Because the team’s got guts. And when it was all over, you cheered and howled and serpentined and felt big as hell. Lord Almighty! you acted as if you’d done something.”

===Chinese===
“橄榄球赛我见得多了，看台上呐喊助威的我也见得多了，可你们这帮家伙，是我见过的最他妈没种、最爱撂挑子的一群孬种。上星期六咱们落后的时候，出了什么事？我问你们；到底出了什么事？你们撂了挑子！像一帮被打怕了的癞皮狗似的撂了挑子。天哪！你们这些胆小鬼，胆小得没治了。可球队照样在拼——而且赢了，不顾你们赢的，替一帮胆小的狗崽子赢的。为什么？因为球队有种。等到一切收了场，你们倒又欢呼、又吼叫、又拉起长蛇阵扭了个痛快，觉得自己了不起得要命。全能的主啊！瞧你们那副样子，倒像真立了什么大功似的。”

===Original===
His right hand came out of his pocket with a jerk, and he extended a fighting, clenched fist toward his breathless audience. “I’ll tell you something,” he said slowly, viciously; “the team can’t win alone day after tomorrow. *It can’t win alone!* You’ve got to fight. Damn it! *You’ve got to fight!* Raleigh’s good, damn good; it hasn’t lost a game this season﻿—and we’ve got to win, *win*! Do you hear? We’ve got to win! And there’s only one way that we can win, and that’s with every man back of the team. Every goddamned mother’s son of you. The team’s good, but it can’t win unless you fight﻿—*fight*!”

===Chinese===
他的右手猛地从裤袋里抽了出来，朝屏息无声的听众伸出一只攥紧的、好斗的拳头。“我告诉你们一件事，”他说得又慢又狠；“后天，球队孤军奋战是赢不了的。*它孤军奋战是赢不了的！*你们得去拼。妈的！*你们必须去拼！*罗利很强，强得要命；这个赛季它一场都没输——而我们必须赢，*赢*！听见没有？我们必须赢！我们要赢，只有一条路：每一个人都站在球队身后撑腰。你们中间每一个人，他妈的一个都不能少。球队是好，可你们不拼，它就赢不了——*拼*！”

===Original===
Suddenly his voice grew softer, almost gentle. He held out both hands to the boys, who had become so tense that they had forgotten to smoke. “We’ve got to win, fellows, for old Sanford. Are you back of us?”

===Chinese===
突然，他的嗓音柔和了下来，几乎带着温情。他向男孩们伸出双手——他们早已紧张得忘了抽烟。“咱们必须赢，伙计们，为了老桑福德。你们给我们撑腰吗？”

===Original===
“Yes!” The tension shattered into a thousand yells. The boys leaped on the chairs and shouted until they could shout no more. When Gifford called for “a regular cheer for Jack Price” and then one for the team﻿—“Make it the biggest you ever gave”﻿—they could respond with only a hoarse croak.

===Chinese===
“愿意！”满场紧绷的张力哗然碎作千百声呐喊。男孩们跳上椅子，喊到再也喊不动为止。等到吉福德号召“给杰克·普赖斯来一支正彩”、接着再给球队来一支——“喊出你们从来没有过的大动静”——他们能回应的，只剩一声嘶哑的嘎叫。

===Original===
Finally the hymn was sung﻿—at least, the boys tried loyally to sing it﻿—and they stood silent and almost reverent as the team filed out of the gymnasium.

===Chinese===
最后，圣歌也唱了——至少，男孩们忠心耿耿地试着唱了——球队鱼贯走出体育馆时，他们肃立无言，近乎虔敬。

===Original===
Hugh walked back to Surrey Hall with several men. No one said a word except a quiet good night as they parted. Carl was in the room when he arrived. He sank into a chair and was silent for a few minutes.

===Chinese===
休同几个人一路走回萨里楼。分手时除了轻轻道一声晚安，谁也没有说话。他回到房里时，卡尔已经在屋里。他一屁股陷进椅子，好几分钟没有作声。

===Original===
Finally he said in a happy whisper, “Wasn’t it wonderful, Carl?”

===Chinese===
末了，他喜不自胜地悄声说：“简直妙极了，卡尔，是不是？”

===Original===
“Un-huh. Damn good.”

===Chinese===
“嗯呐。真他妈棒。”

===Original===
“Gosh, I hope we win. We’ve *got* to!”

===Chinese===
“天哪，真希望咱们能赢。咱们*非*赢不可！”

===Original===
Carl looked up, his cheeks redder than usual, his eyes glittering. “God, yes!” he breathed piously.

===Chinese===
卡尔抬起头来，脸颊比平时更红，眼睛闪闪发亮。“上帝啊，是啊！”他像做祷告似的低声说。

## V / 第五章

===Original===
Capwell Chapel﻿—it bore the pork merchant’s name as an eternal memorial to him﻿—was as impressive inside as out. The stained-glass windows had been made by a famous New York firm; the altar had been designed by an even more famous sculptor. The walls, quite improperly, were adorned with paintings of former presidents, but the largest painting of all﻿—it was fairly Gargantuan﻿—was of the pork merchant, a large, ruddy gentleman, whom the artist, a keen observer, had painted truly﻿—complacently porcine, benevolently smug.

===Chinese===
卡普韦尔礼拜堂（Capwell Chapel）——它冠着那位猪肉商的大名，作为他永久的纪念碑——堂内同堂外一样令人印象深刻。彩绘玻璃窗出自纽约一家著名的作坊；祭坛的设计者是一位名气更大的雕塑家。四壁张挂着历任校长的画像，这实在有失体统；而其中最大的一幅——那尺寸简直够得上高康大（Gargantuan）——画的正是那位猪肉商：一位身材魁伟、面色红润的绅士。画家是个敏锐的观察者，把他画得分毫不爽——一副怡然自得的猪相，一脸和善可亲的自负。

===Original===
The seniors and juniors sat in the nave, the sophomores on the right side of the transept, the freshmen on the left. Hugh gazed upward in awe at the dim recesses of the vaulted ceiling, at the ornately carved choir where gowned students were quietly seating themselves, at the colored light streaming through the beautiful windows, at the picture of the pork merchant. The chapel bells ceased tolling; rich, solemn tones swelled from the organ.

===Chinese===
四年级学生和三年级学生坐在中殿，二年级学生坐在翼廊右侧，新生们坐在左侧。休满怀敬畏地仰望拱顶幽深的穹窿，仰望雕饰华美的唱诗席——身着长袍的学生们正悄然入座——仰望美丽的窗子间倾泻而下的彩色光线，仰望那幅猪肉商的画像。礼拜堂的钟声停了；管风琴涌起浑厚庄严的和鸣。

===Original===
President Culver in cap and gown, his purple hood falling over his shoulders, entered followed by his faculty, also gowned and hooded. The students rose and remained standing until the president and faculty were seated. The organ sounded a final chord, and then the college chaplain rose and prayed﻿—very badly. He implored the Lord to look kindly “on these young men who have come from near and far to drink from this great fount of learning, this well of wisdom.”

===Chinese===
卡尔弗校长（President Culver）头戴方帽、身着长袍，紫色垂布披在肩头，缓步而入，身后跟着全体教员，同样长袍垂布。学生们起立恭候，直到校长和教员们落座。管风琴奏出最后一组和弦，随后校牧起身祷告——祷告得糟糕透了。他恳求主垂怜“这些从四面八方前来，就饮于这座伟大学问之泉、这一口智慧之井的年轻人”。

===Original===
The prayer over, the president addressed the students. He was a large, erect man with iron-gray hair and a rugged intelligent face. Although he was sixty years old, his body was vigorous and free from extra weight. He spoke slowly and impressively, choosing his words with care and enunciating them with great distinctness. His address was for the freshmen: he welcomed them to Sanford College, to its splendid traditions, its high ideals, its noble history. He spoke of the famous men it numbered among its sons, of the work they had done for America and the world, of the work he hoped future Sanford men, they, the freshmen, would some day do for America and the world. He mentioned briefly the boys from Sanford who had died in the World War “to make the world safe for democracy,” and he prayed that their sacrifice had not been in vain. Finally, he spoke of the chapel service, which the students were required to attend. He hoped that they would find inspiration in it, knowledge and strength. He assured them that the service would always be nonsectarian, that there would never be anything in it to offend anyone of any race, creed, or religion. With a last exhortation to the freshmen to make the most of their great opportunities, he ended with the announcement that they would rise and sing the sixty-seventh hymn.

===Chinese===
祷告既毕，校长向学生们致辞。他身材高大，腰背挺直，一头铁灰色的头发，一张粗犷而聪慧的脸。虽已年届六十，身子骨依然矫健，没有一两赘肉。他说话缓慢而有分量，字斟句酌，吐字极其清晰。这篇致辞是说给新生的：他欢迎他们来到桑福德学院（Sanford College），来到它辉煌的传统、它崇高的理想、它高贵的史册。他谈到学院门下养育的名人，谈到他们为美国和世界建立的功业，谈到他期望未来的桑福德人——也就是他们，这些新生——有朝一日能为美国和世界建立的功业。他简略提了提那些在世界大战中“为民主创造一个安全的世界”而捐躯的桑福德子弟，并祈愿他们的牺牲不曾付诸东流。最后，他讲到礼拜仪式，学生们一律必须出席。他希望他们能从中觅得启迪，觅得知识与力量。他向他们保证，礼拜仪式将永远不分教派，其中绝不会有任何开罪任何种族、任何信条、任何宗教之人的东西。临了，他最后一次劝勉新生们善用他们了不起的机遇，随即宣布全体起立，齐唱第六十七首赞美诗。

===Original===
Hugh was deeply impressed by the speech but disturbed by the students. From where he sat he got an excellent view of the juniors and seniors. The seniors, who sat in the front of the nave, seemed to be paying fairly good attention; but the juniors﻿—many of them, at least﻿—paid no attention at all. Some of them were munching apples, some doughnuts, and many of them were reading “The Sanford News,” the college’s daily paper. Some of the juniors talked during the president’s address, and once he noticed four of them doubled up as if overcome by laughter. To him the service was a beautiful and impressive occasion. He could not understand the conduct of the upper-classmen. It seemed, to put it mildly, irreverent.

===Chinese===
这篇演讲令休深深感动，可那些学生令他心神不宁。从他的座位望去，三年级生和四年级生一览无余。坐在中殿前排的四年级生，看模样听得还算用心；可三年级生——至少他们中间的一大半——压根儿就没在听。有几个在大嚼苹果，有几个嚼着炸面圈，还有许多人在读《桑福德新闻》，学院的日报。校长致辞的时候，几个三年级生自顾自讲话，有一回他还看见四个笑得直不起腰来，像乐不可支的样子。在他看来，这场仪式是一个美丽而庄严的时刻。他没法理解高年级学生的行径。说轻些，那未免太不虔敬了。

===Original===
Everyone, however, sang the doxology with great vigor, some of the boys lifting up a “whisky” tenor that made the chapel ring, and to which Hugh happily added his own clear tenor. The benediction was pronounced by the chaplain, the seniors marched out slowly in twos, while the other students and the faculty stood in their places; then the president, followed by the faculty, passed out of the great doors. When the back of the last faculty gown had disappeared, the underclassmen broke for the door, pushing each other aside, swearing when a toe was stepped on, yelling to each other, some of them joyously chanting the doxology. Hugh was caught in the rush and carried along with the mob, feeling ashamed and distressed; this was no way to leave a church.

===Chinese===
不过，唱起《三一颂》来人人都劲头十足，有几个男孩拔起一股“威士忌”男高音，震得满堂嗡嗡回响，休也欢欢喜喜地把自己清亮的男高音加了进去。校牧念了祝福文，四年级生两人一排，缓缓鱼贯而出，其余学生和教员们则站在原处不动；随后校长领头，教员们随后，从大门里走了出去。最后一件学袍的背影消失之后，低年级学生便一齐扑向门口，你推我搡，踩了脚就骂，彼此大呼小叫，有几个还兴高采烈地哼唱着《三一颂》。休被卷进这股人流，身不由己地随众而去，又是羞愧又是难过；离开教堂可不该是这个样子。

===Original===
Once outside, however, he had no time to think of the chapel service; he had five minutes in which to get to his first class, and the building was across the campus, a good two minutes’ walk. He patted his cap to be sure that it was firmly on the back of his head, clutched his notebook, and ran as hard as he could go, the strolling upper-classmen, whom he passed at top speed, grinning after him in tolerant amusement.

===Chinese===
不过一到外面，他就顾不上想礼拜仪式了；他只有五分钟赶到第一堂课，而那座楼在校园另一头，足足要走两分钟。他拍了拍帽子，确信它牢牢扣在后脑勺上，攥紧笔记本，撒腿飞奔，能跑多快跑多快。那些闲庭信步的高年级学生被他一阵风似的一一掠过，望着他的背影咧嘴直乐，又觉有趣，又怀宽容。

===Original===
Hugh was the first one in the classroom and wondered in a moment of panic if he was in the right place. He sat down dubiously and looked at his watch. Four minutes left. He would wait two, and then if nobody came he would﻿—he gasped; he couldn’t imagine what he would do. How could he find the right classroom? Maybe his class didn’t come at this hour at all. Suppose he and Carl had made a mistake. If they had, his whole schedule was probably wrong. “Oh, golly,” he thought, feeling pitifully weak, “won’t that be hell? What can I do?”

===Chinese===
休头一个到了教室，一时心里发慌，拿不准自己走没走对地方。他将信将疑地坐下，看了看表。还剩四分钟。他打算等上两分钟，要是还没有人来，他就——他倒抽了一口气；他想不出自己到时候能怎么办。他怎么才能找对教室呢？没准他那门课根本不在这个钟点上。万一他和卡尔弄错了呢。真弄错了的话，他整张课表恐怕就全错了。“哦，天哪，”他想，觉得自己可怜巴巴地浑身发软，“那岂不是活见鬼？我可怎么办？”

===Original===
At that moment a countrified-looking youth entered, looking as scared as Hugh felt. His face was pale, and his voice trembled as he asked timidly, “Do you know if this is Section Three of Math One?”

===Chinese===
正在这时，一个土里土气的少年走了进来，脸上的惊惶同休心里的不相上下。他脸色发白，怯生生地问道，嗓音直打颤：“请问，这儿是不是数学一（Math One）第三班？”

===Original===
Hugh was immediately strengthened. “I think so,” he replied. “Anyhow, let’s wait and find out.”

===Chinese===
休顿时胆气壮了。“我想是的，”他答道，“不管怎样，等等看，总会弄清楚的。”

===Original===
The freshman sighed in huge relief, took out a not too clean handkerchief, and mopped his face. “Criminy!” he exclaimed as he wriggled down the aisle to a seat by Hugh, “I was sure worried. I thought I was in the wrong building, though I was sure that my adviser had told me positively that Math was in Matthew Six.”

===Chinese===
那新生如释重负，长长出了一口气，掏出一块不算太干净的手帕，抹了抹脸。“我的老天爷！”他一边顺着走道蹭到休身旁的座位上，一边嚷嚷，“我方才真急坏了。还当自己走错了楼呢，虽说我的导师分明一口咬定，数学课在马修楼六号（Matthew Six）。”

===Original===
“I guess we’re all right,” Hugh comforted him as two other freshmen, also looking dubious, entered. They were followed by four more, and then by a stampeding group, all of them pop-eyed, all of them in a rush. In the next minute five freshmen dashed in and then dashed out again, utterly bewildered, obviously terrified, and not knowing where to go or what to do. “Is this Math One, Section Three?” every man demanded of the room as he entered; and everyone yelled, “Yes,” or, “I think so.”

===Chinese===
“我看咱们没走错，”休安慰他道；这时又进来两个新生，也是一脸的将信将疑。随后又来了四个，接着涌进一群横冲直撞的，个个瞪圆了眼睛，个个火烧火燎。再下一分钟，五个新生一头冲进来，又一头冲出去，晕头转向，显然吓得不轻，不知道该往哪儿去、该干什么。“这儿是数学一第三班吗？”每个人一进门就冲着满屋子发问；人人便扯开嗓子答：“是，”或者，“我想是。”

===Original===
Just as the bell rang at ten minutes after the hour, the instructor entered. It was Professor Kane.

===Chinese===
整点过后十分钟，铃声响起，恰在此时，任课教师走了进来。正是凯恩教授（Professor Kane）。

===Original===
“This is Mathematics One, Section Three,” Kane announced in a dry voice. “If there is anyone here who does not belong here, he will please leave.” Nobody moved; so he shuffled some cards in his hand and asked the men to answer to the roll-call.

===Chinese===
“这里是数学一，第三班，”凯恩用干巴巴的嗓音宣布，“若有哪位不属此班，请他出去。”没有人动；于是他把手里的一叠卡片哗啦一洗，叫学生们应点名。

===Original===
“Adams, J. H.”

===Chinese===
“亚当斯，J. H.。”

===Original===
“Present, sir.”

===Chinese===
“到，先生。”

===Original===
Kane looked up and frowned. “Say ‘here,’ ” he said severely. “This is not a grammar-school.”

===Chinese===
凯恩抬起头，皱起眉头。“要说‘到’，”他声色俱厉地说，“这里可不是小学。”

===Original===
“Yes, sir,” stuttered Adams, his face first white then purple. “Here, sir.”

===Chinese===
“是，先生，”亚当斯结结巴巴地说，脸上一阵白一阵紫。“到，先生。”

===Original===
“ ‘Here’ will do; there is no need of the ‘sir.’ Allsop, K. E.”

===Chinese===
“‘到’就行；不必带‘先生’。奥尔索普，K. E.。”

===Original===
“Here”﻿—in a very faint voice.

===Chinese===
“到”——嗓音细得几乎听不见。

===Original===
“Speak up!”

===Chinese===
“大点声！”

===Original===
“Here.” This time a little louder.

===Chinese===
“到。”这回响亮了一点。

===Original===
And so it went, hardly a man escaping without some admonishment. Hugh’s throat went dry; his tongue literally stuck to the roof of his mouth: he was sure that he wouldn’t be able to say “Here” when it came his turn, and he could feel his heart pounding in dreadful anticipation.

===Chinese===
如此一路点将下去，几乎无人能不挨上一句训诫。休的喉咙干得发紧；舌头简直粘在了上颚：他敢肯定，轮到自己的时候，那声“到”是断断喊不出来的；他只觉得心怀着骇人的预感，在胸腔里怦怦狂跳。

===Original===
“Carver, H. M.”

===Chinese===
“卡弗，H. M.。”

===Original===
“Here!”

===Chinese===
“到！”

===Original===
There! it was out! Or had he really said it?

===Chinese===
喊出来了！总算喊出来了！——还是说，他到底喊出声了没有？

===Original===
He looked at the professor in terror, but Kane was already calling, “Dana, R. T.” Hugh sank back in his chair; he was trembling.

===Chinese===
他惊恐地望向教授，可凯恩已经在往下念了：“达纳，R. T.。”休瘫回椅子里；他还在发抖。

===Original===
Kane announced the textbook, and when Hugh caught the word “trigonometry” he actually thrilled with joy. He had had trig in high school. Whoops! Would he hit Math I in the eye? He’d knock it for a goal.﻿ ﻿… Then conscience spoke. Oughtn’t he to tell Kane that he had already had trig? He guessed quite rightly that Kane had not understood his high-school credentials, which had given him credit for “advanced mathematics.” Kane had taken it for granted that that was advanced algebra. Hugh felt that he ought to explain the mistake, but fear of the arid, impersonal man restrained him. Kane had told him to take Math I; and Kane was law.

===Chinese===
凯恩宣布了课本；休一听见“三角学”这个词，竟欢喜得心头一颤。他在中学学过三角。哇哈！他要让“数学一”吃他一记眼拳吗？他要一脚把它轰进大门去。……随后良心开口了。他是不是该告诉凯恩，自己已经学过三角了？他猜得一点不错：凯恩没有弄懂他中学的成绩单——那上面给他记的是“高等数学”的学分。凯恩想当然地以为那是高等代数。休觉得理应把这处误会讲明白，可是对那个干巴巴、不讲情面的人的畏惧使他缩住了。凯恩叫他修数学一；而凯恩的话就是法律。

===Original===
Unlike most of Hugh’s instructors, Kane kept the class the full hour the first day, seating them in alphabetical order﻿—he had to repeat the performance three times during the week as new men entered the class﻿—lecturing them on the need of doing their problems carefully and accurately, and discoursing on the value of mathematics, trigonometry in particular, in the study of science and engineering. Hugh was not interested in science, engineering, or mathematics, but he listened carefully, trying hard to follow Kane’s cold discourse. At the end of the hour he told his neighbor as they left the room that he guessed that Professor Kane knew an awful lot, and his neighbor agreed with him.

===Chinese===
头一天，凯恩便与休的大多数任课教师不同：他把这堂课占满了整整一个钟头——按姓氏字母顺序给学生排定了座位——这一套他在一周之内还不得不重演三回，因为不断有新学生进班——训诫他们做题务必细心、准确，又论述了数学、尤其是三角学在科学与工程研究中的价值。休对科学、工程和数学都不感兴趣，可他听得仔细，竭力跟上凯恩那冷冰冰的讲论。钟点一到，出教室时他跟邻座说，他看凯恩教授懂得的真多；邻座深以为然。

===Original===
Hugh’s other instructors proved less impressive than Kane; in fact, Mr. Alling, the instructor in Latin, was altogether disconcerting.

===Chinese===
休的其他任课教师，比起凯恩来就逊色多了；说实在的，那位拉丁文教师阿林先生（Mr. Alling），简直叫人不知所措。

===Original===
“Plautus,” he told the class, “wrote comedies, farces﻿—not exercises in translation. He was also, my innocents, occasionally naughty﻿—oh, really naughty. What’s worse, he used slang, common everyday slang﻿—the kind of stuff that you and I talk. Now, I have an excellent vocabulary of slang, obscenity, and profanity; and you are going to hear most of it. Think of the opportunity. Don’t think that I mean just ‘damn’ and ‘hell.’ They are good for a laugh in a theater any day, but Plautus was not restrained by our modern conventions. *You* will confine yourselves, please, to English undefiled, but I shall speak the modern equivalent to a Roman gutter-pup’s language whenever necessary. You will find this course very illuminating﻿—in some ways. And, who knows? you may learn something not only about Latin but about Rome.”

===Chinese===
“普劳图斯（Plautus），”他对全班说，“写的是喜剧、笑剧——不是翻译练习。他还有，我的天真的孩子们，时不时地不大正经——哦，真真不大正经。更糟的是，他用俚语，家常的、天天挂嘴上的俚语——就是你我嘴里的那种货色。这么着吧，我在俚语、脏话和渎神话上头，有一副顶呱呱的词汇库；你们将有幸听到其中的一大部分。想想这份好机会吧。别以为我只说‘该死’和‘见鬼’。这两个词哪天在戏院里都能博人一笑，普劳图斯可不受咱们现代礼法的约束。*你们*呢，请务必只说纯净无瑕的英语；而我，必要时会讲一个罗马阴沟小痞子所用语言的现代对应物。你们会发现这门课很有启发性——在某些方面。还有，谁知道呢？你们学到的东西，兴许不止关于拉丁文，还关于罗马。”

===Original===
Hugh thought Mr. Alling was rather flippant and lacking in dignity. Professor Kane was more like a college teacher. Before the term was out he hated Kane with an intensity that astonished him, and he looked forward to his Latin classes with an eagerness of which he was almost ashamed. Plautus in the Alling free and colloquial translations was enormously funny.

===Chinese===
休觉得阿林先生未免轻浮，有失体统。凯恩教授倒更像一位大学教师。学期还没过完，他就恨透了凯恩，那份恨意的强度连他自己都吃惊；而他对拉丁文课的翘首企盼，又热切得让他自己都几乎羞愧。经阿林先生那随意而口语化的转译，普劳图斯实在逗乐极了。

===Original===
Professor Hartley, who gave the history lectures, talked in a bass monotone and never seemed to pause for breath. His words came in a slow steady stream that never rose nor fell nor paused﻿—until the bell rang. The men in the back of the room slept. Hugh was seated near the front; so he drew pictures in his notebook. The English instructor talked about punctuation as if it were very unpleasant but almost religiously important; and what the various lecturers in general science talked about﻿—ten men gave the course﻿—Hugh never knew. In after years all that he could remember about the course was that one man spoke broken English and that a professor of physics had made huge bulbs glow with marvelous colors.

===Chinese===
主讲历史课的哈特利教授（Professor Hartley）用低沉的单调嗓音讲话，仿佛从不需要停下来换气。他的话像一股缓慢而平稳的水流，不涨、不落、不停——直到铃响。坐在教室后排的人睡着了。休的座位靠前；于是他在笔记本上画起小人儿来。英文课教师讲标点符号，那口气仿佛标点虽然极不讨人喜欢，其重要性却近乎神圣；至于普通科学课——那门课由十个人轮着讲——各位讲席究竟讲了些什么，休始终一无所知。多年以后，他对那门课的全部记忆只剩两桩：一个人讲一口结结巴巴的英语，还有一位物理学教授让几只巨大的灯泡发出了奇妙的彩色光。

===Original===
Hugh had one terrifying experience before he finally got settled to his work. It occurred the second day of classes. He was comfortably seated in what he thought was his English class﻿—he had come in just as the bell rang﻿—when the instructor announced that it was a class in French. What was he to do? What would the instructor do if he got up and left the room? What would happen if he didn’t report at his English class? What would happen to him for coming into his English class late? These questions staggered his mind. He was afraid to stay in the French class. Cautiously he got up and began to tiptoe to the door.

===Chinese===
在终于安下心来念书之前，休还经历过一场惊魂。那发生在上课的第二天。他舒舒服服地坐在他以为是英文课的教室里——他恰好踏着铃声进门——任课教师却宣布这是一堂法文课。他该怎么办？要是他起身走出教室，教师会怎样？要是他不去英文课报到，会怎样？要是他上英文课迟到了，又会怎样？这些问题把他弄得晕头转向。他不敢留在法文课上，小心翼翼地站起身，踮着脚尖朝门口挪去。

===Original===
“Wrong room?” the instructor asked pleasantly.

===Chinese===
“走错教室了？”教师和颜悦色地问。

===Original===
Hugh flushed. “Yes, sir.” He stopped dead still, not knowing what to do next.

===Chinese===
休涨红了脸。“是的，先生。”他当场钉住，不知道下一步如何是好。

===Original===
He was a typical rattled freshman, and the class, which was composed of sophomores, laughed. Hugh, angry and humiliated, started for the door, but the instructor held up a hand that silenced the class; then he motioned for Hugh to come to his desk.

===Chinese===
他是那种典型的慌了神的新生；满教室二年级生哄堂大笑。休又气又臊，抬脚便朝门口走，可教师举起一只手，教室顿时鸦雀无声；随后他示意休到他的讲台跟前去。

===Original===
“What class are you looking for?”

===Chinese===
“你要找哪门课的教室？”

===Original===
“English One, sir, Section Seven.” He held out his schedule card, reassured by the instructor’s kindly manner.

===Chinese===
“英语一（English One），先生，第七班。”他递出选课卡，教师和善的态度让他安下心来。

===Original===
The instructor looked at the card and then consulted a printed schedule.

===Chinese===
教师看了看卡片，又查了一张印好的课表。

===Original===
“Oh,” he said, “your adviser made a mistake. He got you into the wrong group list. You belong in Sanders Six.”

===Chinese===
“哦，”他说，“你的导师出了个错。他把你填进了错的分组名单。你该在桑德斯楼六号（Sanders Six）。”

===Original===
“Thank you, sir.” Hugh spoke so softly that the waiting class did not hear him, but the instructor smiled at the intensity of his thanks. As he left the room, he knew that everyone was looking at him; his legs felt as if they were made of wood. It wasn’t until he had closed the door that his knee-joints worked naturally. But the worst was still ahead of him. He had to go to his English class in Sanders 6. He ran across the campus, his heart beating wildly, his hands desperately clenched. When he reached Sanders 6, he found three other freshmen grouped before the door.

===Chinese===
“谢谢您，先生。”休说得很轻，等候上课的全班都没有听见；可从他感激之情的深重上，教师微笑了。走出教室时，他知道人人都在看他；两条腿像木头做的。直到掩上门，他的膝关节才恢复自然。但最难堪的还在前头。他得去桑德斯楼 6 号上英文课。他狂奔着穿过校园，心怦怦乱跳，双手死死攥紧。赶到桑德斯楼 6 号，他发现门口已经聚着另外三个新生。

===Original===
“Is this English One, Section Seven?” one asked tremulously.

===Chinese===
“这儿是英语一第七班吗？”其中一个声音发颤地问。

===Original===
“I think so,” whispered the second. “Do you know?” he asked, turning to Hugh.

===Chinese===
“我想是，”第二个压低嗓音说。“你知道么？”他转向休问。

===Original===
“Yes; I am almost sure.”

===Chinese===
“知道；我差不多能肯定。”

===Original===
They stood there looking at each other, no one quite daring to enter Sanders 6, no one quite daring to leave. Suddenly the front door of the building slammed. A bareheaded youth rushed up the stairs. He was a repeater; that is, a man who had failed the course the preceding year and was taking it over again. He brushed by the scared freshmen, opened the door, and strode into Sanders 6, closing the door behind him.

===Chinese===
他们站在那里面面相觑，谁也不敢太冒失地进桑德斯楼 6 号，谁也不敢抬腿就走。突然，楼的前门砰的一响。一个光着脑袋的青年蹬蹬蹬冲上楼梯。他是个重修生；也就是说，是头一年这门课没及格、这年再修一遍的人。他从吓坏了的新生们身边擦身而过，推开门，大步跨进桑德斯楼 6 号，随手把门带上了。

===Original===
The freshmen looked at each other, and then the one nearest the door opened it. The four of them filed in silently.

===Chinese===
新生们面面相觑，随后离门最近的那个把门推开。四人鱼贯而入，鸦雀无声。

===Original===
The class looked up. “Sit in the back of the room,” said the instructor.

===Chinese===
全班抬起头来。“坐到教室后排去，”教师说。

===Original===
And that was all there was to that. In his senior year Hugh remembered the incident and wondered at his terror. He tried to remember why he had been so badly frightened. He couldn’t; there didn’t seem to be any reason at all.

===Chinese===
事情就这么完了，再无下文。到了四年级，休想起这桩事，诧异于自己当初的惊惶。他试图想起自己何以吓成那样。想不起来了；那惊惶仿佛根本没有任何来由。

## VI / 第六章


===Original===
About a week after the opening of college, Hugh returned to Surrey Hall one night feeling unusually virtuous and happy. He had worked religiously at the library until it had closed at ten, and he had been in the mood to study. His lessons for the next day were all prepared, and prepared well. He had strolled across the moonlit campus, buoyant and happy. Someone was playing the organ in the dark chapel; he paused to listen. Two students passed him, humming softly,

===Chinese===
开学后约莫一个星期，一天夜里，休回到萨里楼（Surrey Hall），心里有一种难得的问心无愧的快乐。他在图书馆一丝不苟地用功到十点闭馆，而且那晚正有读书的心绪。第二天的功课全都备好了，还备得妥妥帖帖。他轻快而欢畅地漫步穿过月色里的校园。有人在黑黢黢的礼拜堂里弹管风琴；他驻足聆听。两个学生从他身边走过，轻轻地哼着，

===Original===
> “Sanford, Sanford, mother of men,
 

 Love us, guard us, hold us true.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
> “桑福德，桑福德，众生之母，
 

 爱我们，护我们，守住我们的真心。……”

===Original===
The dormitories were dim masses broken by rectangles of soft yellow light. Somewhere a banjo twanged. Another student passed.

===Chinese===
宿舍楼是一团团黑影，被一方方柔黄的灯光裁开。什么地方有一把班卓琴铮铮地响。又有一个学生走了过去。

===Original===
“Hello, Carver,” he said pleasantly. “Nice night.”

===Chinese===
“你好啊，卡弗，”他和和气气地说。“晚上真不错。”

===Original===
“Oh, hello, Jones. It sure is.”

===Chinese===
“哦，你好，琼斯。可不是嘛。”

===Original===
The simple greeting completed his happiness. He felt that he belonged, that Sanford, the “mother of men,” had taken him to her heart. The music in the chapel swelled, lyric, passionate﻿—up! up! almost a cry. The moonlight was golden between the heavy shadows of the elms. Tears came into the boy’s eyes; he was melancholy with joy.

===Chinese===
这一声简单的招呼圆满了他的幸福。他觉得自己是这里的人了，觉得桑福德这位“众生之母”已经把他搂进了心怀。礼拜堂里的乐声高涨起来，抒情而炽烈——升上去！升上去！几乎成了一声呼喊。榆树浓重的阴影之间，月光是金色的。泪水涌进了这少年的眼睛；他快乐得近乎怅惘。

===Original===
He climbed the stairs of Surrey slowly, reluctant to reach his room and Carl’s flippancy. He passed an open door and glanced at the men inside the room.

===Chinese===
他缓缓地爬上萨里楼的楼梯，不大情愿这么快就到自己的房间，到卡尔的轻嘴薄舌跟前去。

===Original===
“Hi, Hugh. Come in and bull a while.”

===Chinese===
“嘿，休。进来闲扯一会儿。”

===Original===
“Not tonight, thanks.” He moved on down the hall, feeling a vague resentment; his mood had been broken, shattered.

===Chinese===
“今晚不了，谢谢。”他顺着走廊继续往前走，心里泛起一阵说不清的懊恼；他的心绪被搅了，搅得粉碎。

===Original===
The door opposite his own room was slightly open. A freshman lived there, Herbert Morse, a queer chap with whom Carl and Hugh had succeeded in scraping up only the slightest acquaintance. He was a big fellow, fully six feet, husky and quick. The football coach said that he had the makings of a great halfback, but he had already been fired off the squad because of his irregularity in reporting for practice. Except for what the boys called his standoffishness﻿—some of them said that he was too damned high-hat﻿—he was extremely attractive. He had red, almost copper-colored, hair, and an exquisite skin, as delicate as a child’s. His features were well carved, his nose slightly aquiline﻿—a magnificent looking fellow, almost imperious; or as Hugh once said to Carl, “Morse looks kinda noble.”

===Chinese===
